Settings guide

Every setting Kurooto has, one entry each: what it does, what it can be set to and its default, when you'd change it and what changes when you do, what it costs or sends outward, and what it depends on. Grouped by feature; the contents below lists the groups.

Finding one. On the dashboard, Settings (Alt+S) has a search box that also looks through these entries: type what you want ("night", "calls", "battery") rather than a setting's name. Each setting there shows its one-line tip under its name; click the tip for the whole entry. In the phone app, long-press a setting for its entry. On this page, your browser's find (Ctrl+F) finds a name like brain.main.

Changing one. On the dashboard's Settings page (a feature's settings appear once it's on), or in settings.json in Kurooto's config folder (kurooto paths says where), which holds only what you changed: a dotted name such as brain.main is the key main inside brain, {"brain": {"main": "agy"}}, and deleting a line goes back to the default. Restart Kurooto after editing the file by hand (kurooto stop, then kurooto start). A few settings are set by Kurooto itself (pairing, the call line's address); their entries say so. Secrets (keys, tokens) are never settings: they're in the system keyring.

Times and schedules. A time is 24-hour HH:MM. A schedule is daily 07:30 (several times: daily 07:00,13:00), weekly sun 19:00, hourly, or every 15m / every 3h.

This page is written by tools/gen_settings_guide.py from Kurooto's code and the guide's source (tools/settings_guide/), so every setting is here; the table lists them all on one page.

You and how it talks to you

Who you are to Kurooto (your name, time zone, notes folder) and how its AI words things for you. The persona card's four lines go with every AI request and every call, so the wording is the same on Telegram, in the app and on the phone. They set wording only: a line that would give Kurooto feelings, a backstory or a relationship is dropped, and you're told why. More in Teaching Kurooto about you.

For every setting in this group, unless its entry says otherwise:

  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing by itself. The persona card's lines travel with each request to the AI you chose, like the rest of the request.
  • Depends on: Nothing: these are core settings, always there.

you.name

Your name, as Kurooto uses it in messages and calls.

  • Values: Any text. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Set it at setup or when it gets your name wrong. Messages, the morning page and calls use it from the next one; empty, Kurooto just doesn't use a name.

you.timezone

Your time zone, for every time Kurooto keeps; empty follows this computer's clock.

Reminders, check-ins, the alarm, night hours and every schedule are worked out in this zone.

  • Values: An IANA zone name such as Asia/Kolkata or Europe/Berlin, or empty. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: When this computer's clock is set to another zone than the one you live in (a server, a travel laptop). The schedule reads it when Kurooto starts, so restart it after a change; times already set keep their wall-clock time in the new zone.

you.workspace

Your notes folder: where Kurooto reads and writes notes, journal and course files.

Everything Kurooto writes (journal, course notes, book notes, its own blocks) goes inside it. Obsidian works on it as it is.

  • Values: A full folder path, or empty for Documents/Kurooto. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Set it at setup to the folder you already keep notes in. Changing it later points Kurooto at the new folder from its next start; nothing is moved, so move your notes yourself first.
  • Depends on: Read when Kurooto starts: restart after changing it. Notes sync, the notes brain and the sorter all work in this folder.

you.assistant_name

The name your assistant goes by in messages, calls and the dashboard.

  • Values: Any short name. Default: Kurooto.
  • When to change it: When you'd rather call it something else. Your Telegram bot takes the new name at once and says so; messages, calls and the AI use it from the next one.

you.language

Which language answers come in: the one you wrote in, or always one you name.

  • Values: follow (the language of your message), or a language's name such as English or Hindi. Default: follow.
  • When to change it: When you write in a mix and want answers always in one language. Only the wording of AI answers changes; Kurooto's own fixed messages stay in English.

persona.call_me

What the AI calls you: a nickname, your first name, or nothing.

The dashboard's About you page and /persona change it too.

  • Values: Any text; empty uses your name, or nothing. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: When the AI's way of addressing you feels off. The next answer and the next call use it.

persona.tone

The tone you want answers in, in your own words.

  • Values: A short phrase, e.g. "plain and direct, no pep talk". Empty: the AI's usual tone. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: When answers feel too chirpy, too formal or too long-winded. It shapes wording from the next request; facts and actions don't change.

persona.length

How long answers should be, in your own words.

  • Values: A short phrase, e.g. "short unless I ask for detail". Empty: by the length of your message (see persona.register). Default: empty.
  • When to change it: When answers keep coming out longer or shorter than you like. What you write here wins over the length-matching of persona.register.

persona.avoid

Things answers should never have, such as emojis or motivational lines.

  • Values: A short list in your own words, e.g. "emojis, exclamation marks". Default: empty.
  • When to change it: When something in the AI's style keeps annoying you. The next answer and call leave it out.

persona.register

A short message gets a short answer, a voice note a spoken-style one.

By the message's length only: nothing changes with the time of day or partway through a conversation.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you want the same style of answer whatever you send. A length on your persona card wins over it either way.

persona.templates

Rule-made words (the desk greeting, "Good morning" after "I'm awake", check-in messages) use your persona card's name and avoid line.

The desk greeting says the name only while nobody else is in view, and the name never pushes a part out of it. The avoid line is honoured as punctuation: exclamation marks become full stops, emojis go; nothing they say is dropped. Check-in messages keep their start for the phone's card, so they take the avoid line only.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather the rule-made words stay exactly as they were.

persona.boundaries

Health, relationship and money decisions stay yours: Kurooto names who to ask instead.

Every AI request and call is told so, and a request that asks Kurooto to make such a decision ("should I stop my meds") is answered at once, without the AI, with a short line naming a person to ask. It's a list of rules, not a judgement: anything else goes on as usual.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Leave it on. Turn it off only if the rule keeps catching ordinary questions; then such requests go to the AI like any other.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: rules on this computer. Off, those requests go to your AI like any other.

Your AI

Which AI answers your requests, what stands in when it can't, how long it may work, how the free limits are kept for what you're waiting on, and the small offline helper. Everything an AI does goes through the AI you chose here; a request's words go to that AI's service, and nowhere else. More in AI models.

For every setting in this group, unless its entry says otherwise:

  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Requests go to the AI you chose (agy and Claude Code through their own programs; a Gemini key to Google; Ollama stays on this computer). Changing these settings decides which of them is asked, not how much is sent.
  • Depends on: Nothing: core settings. The dashboard's AI step on the setup page and kurooto ai change the main ones with their checks.

brain.main

The AI that answers your requests and does the bigger tasks.

  • Values: agy, claude, gemini (a key), ollama (on this computer), custom, or an extra Claude account claude:<name>. Empty until setup picks one. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: When you switch AI programs or subscriptions. The next request goes to the new one; requests already running finish where they are. The setup page's AI step does the same with a test first, which is the safer way.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Your requests' words, the files you attach and the notes a request needs go to this AI's service (nothing for ollama). Its free limit or subscription is what's used.
  • Depends on: The program or key for that AI (setup installs and signs in). brain.fallback covers it when it can't answer.

brain.fallback

Which AI takes over when your main one can't answer, in order.

  • Values: One AI's id, a list in order such as ["agy", "gemini"], or empty for the automatic order: at a limit, the other agent program, then a Gemini key. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: When you have a second AI you'd rather use as the stand-in, or none you want used. Empty is right for most people.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: A request that falls back sends the same words to the stand-in's service.
  • Depends on: brain.fallback_on decides what counts as "can't answer".

brain.fallback_on

What makes a request move on to the stand-in AI: a limit, a sign-in problem or a timeout.

  • Values: A list of rate_limited, auth, timeout; [] never falls back. Default: ["rate_limited"].
  • When to change it: Add timeout if your main AI is often slow, auth if its sign-in often lapses. Empty it if you'd rather wait than have another AI answer.
  • Depends on: brain.fallback.

brain.classify

Who decides how hard a request is (and so how much effort it gets).

  • Values: rules (no AI), main (asks your AI first), or ollama (a model on this computer). Default: rules.
  • When to change it: rules is quick and free. Pick ollama if you have a local model and the rules misjudge your requests; main costs one small extra request each time.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: main sends each request's text to your AI once more, to rate it; rules and ollama stay here.

brain.timeout_min.low

Minutes a quick request may run before it's stopped.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 10.
  • When to change it: Raise it if quick requests get cut off on a slow connection; lower it to give up sooner on stuck ones.

brain.timeout_min.medium

Minutes a normal request may run before it's stopped.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 15.
  • When to change it: Raise it if ordinary requests (reading a few notes, a short write-up) get cut off.

brain.timeout_min.high

Minutes a hard request may run before it's stopped.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 30.
  • When to change it: Raise it for long jobs (a whole chapter, a big project change) that get cut off; a stopped request says so and can be asked again.

brain.models

Which model each AI uses at each effort level, where you want other than its preset.

  • Values: A JSON object per AI, e.g. {"custom": {"low": "...", "medium": "...", "high": "..."}} or {"claude": {"high": "opus"}}. Default: none.
  • When to change it: When you want a cheaper or stronger model at one level. Settings doesn't show it: the AI step on the setup page and kurooto ai set it, with a test.

brain.base_urls

Another address for an AI's service, in place of its preset.

  • Values: A JSON object, e.g. {"custom": "http://localhost:8000/v1"}. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Only for custom (an OpenAI-style server of your own) or a proxy. Set through the setup page's AI step, which tests it.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Requests go to the address you give, so make sure it's one you trust.

brain.claude_accounts

Your extra Claude accounts, each its own AI you can pick (claude:<name>).

  • Values: Set by Kurooto when you add an account: {"work": {"name": "Work"}}. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Add one on the setup page (AI → Claude Code → Add another Claude account) or with kurooto ai add-claude NAME; rename or remove it in Settings → Claude accounts. Don't edit it by hand.

brain.effort_map

Effort changes Dreaming learned for your requests, e.g. low ones run at medium.

  • Values: A JSON object from one effort to another: {"low": "medium"}. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Set by Dreaming's nightly replay, one change at a time with Undo. Empty it to go back to the efforts as rated.
  • Depends on: Dreaming with dreaming.tune_effort on.

brain.fallback_first

Effort levels at which your first stand-in AI is tried before the main one (learned by Dreaming).

  • Values: A list of low, medium, high. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Set by Dreaming when its replay shows the stand-in does better at a level. Empty it to always start with the main AI.
  • Depends on: Dreaming with dreaming.tune_fallbacks on, and a brain.fallback.

brain.agy.io_mode

How Kurooto talks to the agy program: through a pipe, or a pseudo-terminal.

  • Values: One of: pipe; pty (a pseudo-terminal, where pipes give no output). Default: pipe.
  • When to change it: Only if agy runs but Kurooto gets no output from it (some versions buffer when piped): try pty.
  • Depends on: agy as your main or fallback AI.

brain.claude.session

Keep Claude Code running between turns of a conversation, so each message doesn't start it again.

Your chat with Kurooto (Telegram, the phone app's chats, voice) keeps one Claude Code program running and sends it each message, instead of starting it for every request, so its answers start sooner. It gets exactly the permissions, tools, folders and model a fresh run would, and a request that needs other ones gets its own; it remembers the conversation itself. It closes after brain.claude.session_idle_min, when your AI or this switch changes, when the chat starts over (/new) and when Kurooto stops; if it fails, that message is answered by a fresh run.

  • Values: true (default) or false. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if a kept Claude Code misbehaves, or to have every message start Claude Code fresh as before 0.8.17.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing more is sent: the same messages go to Anthropic through Claude Code. A kept program uses some memory while it waits (at most two).
  • Depends on: Claude Code as your AI (brain.main claude or claude:<name>).

brain.claude.session_idle_min

Minutes a kept Claude Code waits for your next message before it closes.

  • Values: Minutes, more than 0. Default: 10.
  • When to change it: Longer keeps it for slow conversations; shorter frees its memory sooner.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing is sent; a waiting program uses memory.
  • Depends on: brain.claude.session.

quota.on

Keep part of each AI's free limit for what you're waiting on, so background work never uses it all.

Background work (digests, nightly reading) waits once a day's count reaches quota.background_pct of a known limit. Your own requests never wait.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off on a paid plan with room to spare: background work then never waits. Counting and the usage line stay.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: counts and times only, in quota.json on this computer.

quota.background_pct

The share of an AI's daily limit background work may use before it waits for the reset.

  • Values: A percentage, 0 to 100. Default: 70.
  • When to change it: Lower it if you run out before evening; raise it if background work waits while you hardly use the AI yourself.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: counts and times only, in quota.json on this computer.
  • Depends on: quota.on.

quota.limits

Your own daily limits per AI, which win over the ones Kurooto learned.

  • Values: A JSON object: {"agy": 900, "gemini:gemini-flash-latest": 250}. Default: none.
  • When to change it: When you know your plan's real limit and the learned one is wrong. Kurooto then keeps its reserve against your number.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: counts and times only, in quota.json on this computer.
  • Depends on: quota.on.

quota.soft

Whether a limit Kurooto only measured makes background work wait, or is just shown.

  • Values: learned (a limit learned from its own count is a measure only), all (Google's numbers too), off (every known limit keeps its reserve, as before 0.7.5). Default: learned.
  • When to change it: Pick off if background work keeps using up your day before you need the AI. A "limit reached" answer always pauses that AI until its reset, whatever this says.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: counts and times only, in quota.json on this computer.
  • Depends on: quota.on.

quota.local

What a model on this computer takes over to spare the free limit.

  • Values: quick (only the "how hard is this" question), background (queued background work too, instead of waiting), off. Default: quick.
  • When to change it: background keeps digests coming on a tight limit, written by a smaller model (weaker). off if you have no local model.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves: the local model runs here; it uses this computer's memory and graphics card while it works.
  • Depends on: Local AI models on, with a model installed.

jobs.receipt

One quiet line under each answer: which AI, effort, time, limit used and what it did.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't care which AI answered. Nothing else changes; the AI jobs page keeps the same record.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing.

jobs.header

A first line on each answer naming the AI and repeating what you asked.

On (the default): "[Claude Code · medium] what you asked" comes first, then the answer. Off: the answer alone. A failed request keeps its first line either way, so you know what failed.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off for shorter messages, above all in the phone app.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing.

jobs.plan_line

Before a long answer, one short line saying what it's about to do and roughly how long.

Only when your last requests of the same kind took more than about 20 seconds. Never on a call.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather get just the answer.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: worked out from what's known before the request runs.

jobs.stuck_min

Minutes a request may sit with no AI at work and no program running before the watchdog stops it.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 10.
  • When to change it: Raise it if long-thinking requests are stopped while they're still fine; lower it to free a stuck queue sooner. A stopped request says so.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing.

tools.latex

Your AI can compile a LaTeX file to a PDF with the compile_latex tool (shell escape off).

Kurooto compiles a copy of the .tex (Tectonic, else latexmk or pdflatex, at most five minutes) and answers with the PDF's path or the first errors. The PDF goes beside the .tex in your files folder (files.folder) or the course files folder, else into the files folder's inbox; never into your notes when a files folder is set. With a files folder, your AI is told to write the documents it makes there.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if your AI shouldn't make PDFs.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves this computer (Tectonic fetches LaTeX packages it lacks the first time).
  • Depends on: LaTeX on this computer (TeX Live, MiKTeX, MacTeX) or Tectonic.

agents.export

Which other AI programs get your skills as slash commands, so /journal works there too.

  • Values: A list of agy, agents (Copilot CLI and others that read ~/.agents) and claude (Claude Code); empty: none. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Add a program you use directly. Its skills folder gets a copy of each skill (marked as Kurooto's; yours are never touched), kept up to date every hour. Emptying it removes every copy Kurooto made.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves: files written into those programs' own skills folders on this computer.
  • Depends on: Those programs installed. kurooto export-skills on|off changes it too.

agents.prefix

A prefix put before every skill copied into your other AI programs, e.g. k- for /k-journal.

  • Values: Short text, or empty. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: When a copied skill's name clashes with one of the program's own commands. The copies are renamed within the hour.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing.
  • Depends on: agents.export.

agents.scripts

Scripts of your own the AI programs may run during a request (full paths).

  • Values: A list of full paths. Default: none.
  • When to change it: When you want a request to be able to use a tool you wrote (say, make-figures). Everything else stays refused; add only scripts you trust to run unattended.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: The scripts run as you on this computer when the AI calls them; what they do is up to them.
  • Depends on: agy or Claude Code as the AI.

local_helper.on

A small model on this computer (about 1 GB) for quick decisions and as a stand-in while the internet is down.

It rates how hard a request is, picks between close readings of a short message and reads a short yes/no. Never answers your requests while you're online.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on (or /helper on) on a computer without a graphics card when the free limit runs short or the internet often drops. It only stays on if it passes a speed test here.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: One download from Ollama's registry (about 1 GB of disk). Nothing else leaves; it uses some memory while it runs.
  • Depends on: Ollama (installed for you).

local_helper.model

Which Ollama model the offline helper uses; empty picks the standard small one.

  • Values: An Ollama model name, or empty. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Only if you want a different small model; it must still pass the speed test.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Downloads that model from Ollama's registry.
  • Depends on: local_helper.on.

local_helper.timeout_s

Seconds a quick decision waits for the helper before the rules decide instead.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 4.
  • When to change it: Raise it on a slow computer if the helper keeps timing out; the rules are always the fallback.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing.
  • Depends on: local_helper.on.

local_helper.max_s

How fast the helper must answer in its speed test (seconds), or it stays off.

  • Values: A number. Default: 1.5.
  • When to change it: Raise it to let a slower computer use the helper anyway; decisions will feel slower.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing.
  • Depends on: local_helper.on.

local_helper.offline

With the internet down: quick things answered here, AI requests wait in the queue until it's back.

You're told once that a request waits; when the connection is back, one line says what's running. Nothing is dropped silently.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to have requests fail at once while offline instead of waiting.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: nothing extra is pinged to find out the connection is down.

local_helper.hold_h

Hours a request waits for the internet before it gives up and says so.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 24.
  • When to change it: Lower it if old requests coming back much later would be useless; raise it for long trips offline.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing.
  • Depends on: local_helper.offline.

local_helper.offer

Offer the offline helper once if your free limit keeps running short or the internet often drops.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you never want to be asked. /helper on works either way.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: one message with Install / Not now, two weeks after setup, and only when it fits.

chat.context_line

Each message carries one line for your AI on how it came: typed where, a call, stepped away from the desk.

Plain words only: typed on Telegram, in the phone app or on this computer; whether you're on a call; and, with Face presence noticing when you leave, that you stepped away from the desk and when. Spoken turns have their own line (who Kurooto recognised). Never a place unless chat.context_places is on.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to send only what you typed.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: One line more in each request to the AI you picked.

chat.context_places

Add your phone's last saved place and the Wi-Fi's name you gave it to that line.

From Brain's phone senses (the places you saved in the phone app, and Wi-Fi codes you labelled with /senses wifi label): "Their phone left the place they saved as home (14:10)". Labels only, never a position. Off by default: the place names go to the AI you picked.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you want answers that know where you are, and are happy for your AI to see those labels.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Your place and Wi-Fi labels go to the AI you picked with each message.
  • Depends on: Brain, and its phone places or Wi-Fi sensor.

Messages and where they reach you

Where Kurooto's messages go (Telegram, the phone app, this computer's notifications), how many come by themselves in a day (Calm), the offline and back-online messages, and questions waiting for your answer. Answers to what you asked are never held or counted; the alarm, approvals, your own reminders and deadlines under 12 hours never wait. More in Messages Kurooto sends by itself.

For every setting in this group, unless its entry says otherwise:

  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Messages go out through Telegram's servers to your chat, or sealed through your call line to the phone app; desktop notifications stay on this computer. Calm's records (state/calm.db, 45 days) stay here.
  • Depends on: Nothing: core settings, always there.

notify.calm.on

Calm: each message Kurooto sends by itself says why, has Why? and Less of this, and there's a daily limit.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to have everything come as it happens, with no buttons and nothing recorded, exactly as before Calm existed.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves. On, what was sent and why is recorded on this computer for 45 days.

notify.calm.messages

How many messages a day may come by themselves; the rest wait for the evening bundle.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 6.
  • When to change it: Raise it if useful things keep waiting until evening; lower it for a quieter day. The first time a day the limit holds one, a line says so with Send them as they come today / Raise to 12 / Fine.
  • Depends on: notify.calm.on.

notify.calm.calls

How many calls a day may come by themselves; one past it waits in the evening bundle as a call link.

  • Values: A whole number; 0: none by themselves. Default: 1.
  • When to change it: Raise it if you like being called for reminders and check-ins; set 0 to be called only when you asked. The alarm and calls you asked for never count.
  • Depends on: notify.calm.on, Phone calls.

notify.calm.evening_at

When the evening bundle comes with everything that waited today.

  • Values: A time of day, 24-hour HH:MM. Default: 20:00.
  • When to change it: Move it to when you usually look at your phone in the evening. After this time, messages come as they are.
  • Depends on: notify.calm.on.

notify.calm.bundle_one

Send the evening bundle as one long message instead of one by one.

Off (the default since 0.8.17): the bundle is a short header, then each message that waited, by itself, in order, with its own buttons, so replying to one or tapping its buttons works as if it had come at once. On: one message (split only where Telegram's length limit makes it), every item numbered and its buttons numbered to match, as before 0.8.17; the phone app's chat shows six buttons under a message, and the rest go on in follow-up messages.

  • Values: false (default) or true. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if a busy evening fills your chat with too many separate messages.
  • Depends on: notify.calm.on.

notify.calm.evening

Kinds of message that always wait for the evening bundle.

  • Values: A list of message kinds, e.g. ["research", "weekly"]; filled by Less of this → Another time. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Usually set by tapping Less of this → Another time. Remove a kind to get it as it happens again.
  • Depends on: notify.calm.on.

notify.calm.fewer

Kinds of message limited to a number a day; more wait for the evening.

  • Values: A JSON object, kind → number: {"quiz": 1}; filled by Less of this → Fewer. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Usually set by tapping Less of this → Fewer. Raise or remove a kind's number to get more of it.
  • Depends on: notify.calm.on.

notify.calm.habituate

After this many of a kind go unanswered in a row, they wait for the evening until you answer one.

  • Values: A JSON object, kind → number; set by Dreaming, with Undo. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Dreaming fills it in from what you leave unanswered. Remove a kind to have it always come as it happens.
  • Depends on: notify.calm.on; Dreaming tunes it.

notify.calm.windows

Hours a kind of message may come in; outside them it waits for the evening bundle.

  • Values: A JSON object, kind → "10:00-18:00"; set by Dreaming, with Undo. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Dreaming sets it from when you actually answer; edit or remove a kind to change its hours.
  • Depends on: notify.calm.on; Dreaming tunes it.

notify.calm.stopped

Kinds of message you stopped with Less of this → Stop.

  • Values: A list of kinds. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Remove a kind to start getting it again (or use Undo on the message that stopped it).
  • Depends on: notify.calm.on. Used only for kinds whose feature has no switch of its own.

notify.calm.recheck

A message that waited says what changed meanwhile when it goes out ("done 14:10", "class skipped").

It applies wherever a message waited: the evening bundle, the end of focus time, the morning after Kurooto slept, the network coming back, Brain's door. It reads the deadline list and the day's skipped classes.

  • Values: rewrite: the line is added and the message still comes; drop: such a message is left out; false: it comes as it was written. Default: rewrite.
  • When to change it: Set drop if you'd rather not see reminders about something already done; false for messages exactly as they were.
  • Depends on: notify.calm.on.

notify.calm.talk

Tune these messages in plain words: "why did you send that?", "less of this", "send me what's waiting".

The same as the Why? and Less of this buttons, the evening bundle and /calm. A change made this way comes with an Undo on your chat. On a call the AI gets four tools for it.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if these words should go to your AI instead.
  • Depends on: notify.calm.on.

notify.calm.night_bedside

With bedside mode open on your phone at the evening bundle's time, the bundle waits until it closes.

You're in bed already: what waited today comes when bedside mode closes (usually the morning).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if the evening bundle reaches you in bed.
  • Depends on: A paired Android phone and phone.bedside.signal.

notify.calm.lighter_after

After an exam or a heavy day, the next day's limit is halved.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather have the same flow every day.
  • Depends on: notify.calm.on.

notify.calm.monthly

Once a month, it names a kind of question you hardly ever answer and offers to turn it off.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't want the monthly suggestion. Nothing is turned off without your tap either way.
  • Depends on: notify.calm.on.

notify.presence.on

Tell you when Kurooto goes offline, and again when it's back with what it caught up on.

A clean stop says so before it exits; coming back says how long it was away and what it caught up on. Never a call; at night it waits for the morning.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't care when the computer sleeps or restarts.

notify.presence.after_min

How long Kurooto must be away before going offline or coming back is worth a message.

  • Values: Minutes, at least 1. Default: 10.
  • When to change it: Raise it if short sleeps and network blips cause messages you don't need.
  • Depends on: notify.presence.on.

notify.presence.channels

Where the offline and back-online messages go.

  • Values: A list of telegram and app (the phone app). Default: ["telegram"].
  • When to change it: Add app if you mostly use the phone app; remove telegram to keep them off your chat.
  • Depends on: notify.presence.on; a paired phone for app.

notify.presence.line

While this computer can't speak (asleep, crashed, offline), your call line tells you so.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't want messages from the call line while the computer is away.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Your call line (on your own Cloudflare account) watches the computer's link and sends the message itself.
  • Depends on: notify.presence.on and a call line (Phone calls).

notify.presence.line_telegram

Whether your call line may keep your bot's token, so it can tell you on Telegram while the computer is away.

  • Values: none (asked once), on, or off (off deletes the token there at the next sync, within 5 minutes). Default: none.
  • When to change it: Answer the question when it comes; change it later here. Off, the call line can only reach the phone app.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: On, your bot's token is kept on your call line (your own Cloudflare account), so it can send to your chat.
  • Depends on: notify.presence.line.

notify.desktop

Pop-up notifications on this computer.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you only want messages on your phone. A message that no other channel took still pops up.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves: the system's own notifications.

notify.desktop_kinds

Which kinds of message pop up on this computer.

  • Values: A list of kinds: call, question, approval, deadline, update, reminder, result, info. Default: ["call", "question", "approval", "deadline", "update"].
  • When to change it: Add reminder if you sit at this computer all day; remove kinds that distract you.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves.
  • Depends on: notify.desktop.

notify.app_kinds

Which kinds of message also reach your phone app.

  • Values: A list of kinds: call, question, approval, plus reminder, deadline, result, info if you want those. Default: ["call", "question", "approval"].
  • When to change it: Add kinds you want on the phone too; remove question and approval to keep those on Telegram only.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Each one goes sealed with your phone's key through your call line, which can't read it.
  • Depends on: A paired phone app.

notify.app_chat

Kurooto's own messages also show in the phone app's chat, not just on Telegram.

Everything not already in notify.app_kinds: reminders, results, information.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on where Telegram is blocked or you'd rather use the app's chat.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed with your phone's key through your call line.
  • Depends on: A paired phone app.

notify.follow_me

Follow me: while Face presence says you've stepped away, messages that pop up on the desktop also go to the phone app.

The desktop pop-up still waits for you when face.away.hold_desktop is on, and Telegram gets everything as usual. Kinds the app already gets (notify.app_kinds) don't come twice. With Face presence off it can't tell where you are, and nothing changes.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you walk away from the computer with the phone and want the desktop's messages there too.
  • Depends on: Face presence (face.away.on) and a paired phone app.

notify.desk_silent_phone

While you're at the desk, Telegram's copy comes without a sound (urgent ones, calls and approvals still ring).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if the phone buzzing beside the screen that already shows the message bothers you.
  • Depends on: notify.follow_me and Face presence.

notify.desk_first

While you're at the desk, every message also pops up on the desktop, not only notify.desktop_kinds.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on to see everything on the screen in front of you first.
  • Depends on: notify.follow_me, notify.desktop and Face presence.

notify.guests_hold

With someone else at the desk, the desktop pop-up only says "A message from Kurooto".

Someone else means two faces close and looking at the screen (Face presence) or a voice that isn't yours lately (Voice ID). Only counts are used, never who. Nothing is held or dropped.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if people often sit with you at this computer.
  • Depends on: Face presence or Voice ID to notice others.

notify.respect_phone_dnd

While your phone is on Do Not Disturb, what isn't urgent waits (as during focus) and calls Kurooto places by itself wait too.

From the phone's Do Not Disturb sense (Brain). What waited goes when Do Not Disturb ends, or when the phone stops saying so for 15 minutes. The alarm, your own reminders, approvals, deadlines under 12 hours and anything urgent still come. Used on this computer only.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on so Kurooto respects the phone's quiet.
  • Depends on: Brain and the phone's Do Not Disturb sense (mind.senses.phone.dnd).

notify.desktop_retract

A desktop pop-up for a question closes once you've answered it anywhere, and one held while you were away doesn't show.

On Linux the pop-up is closed through the desktop's notification service; on Windows and macOS pop-ups stay (best effort).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep every pop-up until you close it.

ask.expire_hours

Hours an unanswered question stays open before it expires (unless its asker set another time).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 48.
  • When to change it: Raise it if you often answer days later; lower it so old questions stop lingering.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing.

ask.keep_days

Days answered and expired questions are kept before they're forgotten.

  • Values: Days; 0 follows privacy.keep_days, and if that's 0 too, 30 days. Default: 0.
  • When to change it: Lower it to forget your answers sooner; raise it if the weekly look back should see more of them.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: kept in the state folder on this computer.

telegram.menu

The bot's "/" menu lists the commands of the features you have on.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather keep a menu you set yourself in BotFather: Kurooto removes its own and leaves the menu alone.
  • Depends on: Telegram.

telegram.silent_kinds

Kinds of message sent to Telegram without a sound.

  • Values: A list of kinds. Default: ["call"].
  • When to change it: Add kinds you don't want buzzing your phone twice. Only while a phone app is paired (it rings by itself).
  • Depends on: Telegram and a paired phone app.

telegram.chat_id

Your chat with the bot (set when you pair; don't change by hand).

  • Values: Text. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Never by hand: pairing sets it. Pair again (kurooto telegram) to move to another chat.
  • Depends on: Telegram. Not shown on the dashboard.

telegram.allowed_users

The Telegram accounts that may use your bot (set when you pair).

  • Values: A list of Telegram user ids. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Set by pairing. Anyone not on it is ignored, so don't add accounts you don't own.
  • Depends on: Telegram. Not shown on the dashboard.

telegram.api_base

Telegram's API address. Leave it alone.

  • Values: Text. Default: https://api.telegram.org.
  • When to change it: Only for tests or a Bot API server of your own.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Every Telegram message goes to this address.
  • Depends on: Telegram. Not shown on the dashboard.

keep.ask

On day 10, then at most monthly: Keep / Quiet / Off for features you don't use.

From use counted on this computer. Nothing is turned down without your tap.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you never want the question. /keep shows the counts either way.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: counted on this computer.

turnon.offer

When you ask for something a feature that's off would do, offer to turn it on.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to have such requests go straight to your AI instead.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: rules only.
  • Depends on: Telegram or the phone app (where a button can be tapped).

repair.on

When a short request could mean either of two things, ask which with both as buttons.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather it went to your AI than asked.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: rules on this computer.

notify.calm.voice_stops

"Stop" said over something Kurooto said aloud by itself is noted; after a few, it offers to stop that kind.

Each stop over a greeting, an answer read aloud from elsewhere or an add-on's line is a mark for that kind (never your words). Three in two weeks bring one message asking whether to stop them; only your tap does, with Undo.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to have stops only stop it there and then.

When it may interrupt you

The night, focus time, calendar events that hold calls, and the regular commitments in your week. A held call or message isn't lost: it comes once the hold ends (or in the evening bundle). Learned changes to your hours are said once, with Undo, and never touch hours you set yourself. More in Phone calls and the morning alarm.

For every setting in this group, unless its entry says otherwise:

  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: rules on this computer. What it learns about your hours (two times) is kept in state/rhythm.json.
  • Depends on: Nothing: core settings, always there.

availability.night.enabled

No calls during the night hours.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off only if calls at any hour are fine with you. With the wake-up alarm set, the night runs from your bedtime to the alarm instead.

availability.night.from

When the night hours start.

  • Values: A time of day, 24-hour HH:MM. Default: 23:30.
  • When to change it: Set it to when you usually go to sleep. Kurooto may move it for you (availability.learn_night) unless you set it here yourself.

availability.night.to

When the night hours end.

  • Values: A time of day, 24-hour HH:MM. Default: 07:00.
  • When to change it: Set it to when you're usually up. Once you set it, the learning leaves it alone.

availability.night.alarm_only

Hold calls at night only on nights you've set a wake-up alarm.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you set the alarm every night you want quiet, and calls on other nights are fine.
  • Depends on: The Morning alarm.

availability.night.phone_alarm

Your phone's own alarm ends the night's call hold, when it's earlier than the night's end (never later).

With the phone's alarm sense on (Brain), a 06:00 alarm on the phone ends the night at 06:00 instead of 07:00, and once it has rung the night is over. When it and Kurooto's alarm disagree for the same morning, Kurooto asks once whether to keep both; neither alarm changes without your tap. The phone's alarm time is used on this computer only.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep the night hours as set, whatever the phone's alarm says.
  • Depends on: Brain and the phone's alarm sense (mind.senses.phone.alarm).

availability.learn_night

Move the night hours to when you're actually quiet, learned from three weeks of your activity, with Undo.

At most once a week, never night hours you set yourself, and not for a month after an Undo.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if your hours vary a lot or you want them fixed.

availability.learn_morning

The check-in day, the morning message and "Time to start" follow your learned hours.

Unless you set those times yourself: the check-in day turns two hours before you're up, the morning message comes half an hour after, Time to start an hour after.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep those at their clock times.
  • Depends on: availability.learn_night.

availability.absence_days

After this many days with nothing from you, one gentle "all good?" message.

  • Values: Days; 0: never. Default: 2.
  • When to change it: Set 0 if you often go quiet for days on purpose. It's never repeated, never a call, and Busy gives you a quiet week.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: One Telegram message, through Calm.

availability.focus_keyword

A calendar event with this word in its title is focus time: messages and calls wait.

  • Values: A word, e.g. #focus. Default: #focus.
  • When to change it: Change it if you already use another tag for deep-work blocks.
  • Depends on: The Calendar feature.

availability.nocall_keyword

A calendar event with this word in its title holds calls while it's on.

  • Values: A word, e.g. #nocall. Default: #nocall.
  • When to change it: Change it to a tag you already use. Matters when calendar.nocall_events is tagged.
  • Depends on: The Calendar feature.

availability.nocall_calendar

Part of a calendar's name: every event in such a calendar holds calls.

  • Values: Part of a calendar's name, any case. Default: no call.
  • When to change it: Name a calendar (say, "Shifts") whose events should always hold calls. Matters when calendar.nocall_events is tagged.
  • Depends on: The Calendar feature.

commitments.items

Your regular commitments (the gym, a shift, a standup), each on its days and hours.

  • Values: A list of {"name": "gym", "days": ["mon", "wed"], "start": "18:00", "end": "19:00", "calls": true}. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Easier with /busy add gym mon,wed 18:00-19:00 or by saying "every Tuesday at 7pm I go to the gym". Edit here to fix a time.
  • Depends on: The Commitments feature.

commitments.no_calls

No calls while a commitment is on.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep commitments only as a list. Each one can also opt out alone: /busy calls gym off.
  • Depends on: The Commitments feature.

commitments.hooks

Words of yours that start a hold: "gym" alone holds calls for 90 minutes.

  • Values: A list of {"word": "gym", "hold": "calls" or "all", "minutes": 90}. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Set by saying "when I say gym, hold calls 90 min"; /hooks drop gym forgets one. A hook never sets an alarm or sends anything.
  • Depends on: The Commitments feature.

Phone calls and the call line

Calls both ways through your own call line (a small Cloudflare Worker on your account), the voice they use, how persistently they ring, the look back after each call, what the phone app gets from the computer, and reminders that still arrive while this computer's lid is shut. More in Phone calls and the morning alarm.

For every setting in this group, unless its entry says otherwise:

  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Calls run through your call line on your own Cloudflare account. Live calls stream your voice to Google's Gemini Live with your key; turn-by-turn calls send each turn's text to Gemini (or stay here with a local model). What goes to the phone app is sealed with its key.
  • Depends on: The Phone calls feature, with its call line deployed and a phone paired.

calls.voice_mode

How calls listen and speak: Gemini Live, your phone's own speech turn by turn, or Live with the phone as backup.

  • Values: auto (Live, and the phone's speech when Live fails), live (Live only; a failed call goes to Telegram), phone (the phone's speech every time). Default: auto.
  • When to change it: Pick phone if Live is often down or you're on a weak connection; live if you never want the walkie-talkie style. Applies from the next call.

calls.voice_by_kind

The voice mode for one kind of call, e.g. the alarm always on the phone's speech.

  • Values: A JSON object, kind → mode: {"alarm": "phone", "checkins:evening": "live"}. Kinds: a feature (alarm, checkins, weekly, quiz, deadlines, research), one of its calls (checkins:night), reminder, outgoing. Default: none.
  • When to change it: When one kind of call works better another way (a sleepy alarm call on the plainer phone speech, say). The more exact kind wins over calls.voice_mode.

calls.live_model

The Gemini Live model calls try first.

  • Values: A Gemini Live model name. Default: gemini-3.8-live.
  • When to change it: When Google brings out a newer Live model, or this one is retired. A model that fails moves the call to the next in calls.live_models.

calls.live_models

Other Live models to try, in order, when the first one is down.

  • Values: A list of model names; [] keeps to calls.live_model. Default: ["gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview", "gemini-2.5-flash-native-audio-latest"].
  • When to change it: Add a model you know works on your key; empty it if you'd rather fall back to turn by turn than to an older model.

calls.voice

Which Gemini Live voice calls speak in; empty is Google's default.

  • Values: A Live voice name such as Kore, Puck or Charon. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: When you'd like a different voice. Takes effect on the next call.

calls.voice_by_model

A voice for each Live model, where a model doesn't know the one in calls.voice.

  • Values: A JSON object, model → voice name. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Only if a fallback model refuses your chosen voice.

calls.turn_brain

Who answers turn-by-turn calls on your computer: Gemini with your key, or a local model.

  • Values: One of: gemini (your key); local (Local AI models). Default: gemini.
  • When to change it: Pick local to keep turn-by-turn calls entirely on this computer (needs a capable local model; answers are slower and plainer).
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: gemini sends each turn's words to Google with your key; local keeps them here.

calls.text_model

The Gemini model that answers turn-by-turn calls (here, or on your call line while the computer is off).

  • Values: A Gemini model name. Default: gemini-flash-latest.
  • When to change it: When a cheaper or faster model suits you, or this one is retired.

calls.persona

Your own words on how calls should sound, such as "be brief and a bit cheeky".

  • Values: Text. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: When calls feel too chatty or too stiff. Your persona card applies to calls too; this adds call-only wording.

calls.ring_s

Seconds a call rings before it counts as missed (a wake-up call rings 5 minutes).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 60.
  • When to change it: Raise it if you often reach the phone just too late.

calls.realert_min

Minutes after the first ring that an unanswered call rings again.

  • Values: A list of minutes, e.g. [3, 6]; [] rings once. Default: [3, 6].
  • When to change it: Empty it if one ring is enough; add a later one if you sleep through calls you asked for.

calls.hold_moving

Calls Kurooto places by itself wait while your phone says you're walking or in a vehicle.

From the phone's motion sense (Brain), checked again every 10 minutes. Calls you asked for, the alarm and deadlines less than 12 hours away still ring. Headphones never hold a call. Used on this computer only, never sent to an AI.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if calls on the way to class or on the road are a nuisance.
  • Depends on: Brain and the phone's motion sense (mind.senses.phone.motion).

calls.phone_busy

Calls Kurooto places by itself, and their retries, wait while the phone app says it's in another call (WhatsApp, Meet).

The app sends only until when it's busy, never who you're talking to. Kept apart from Kurooto's own calls, so desk listening and the greeting go on unless voice.listen.phone_call is on.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on so Kurooto doesn't keep retrying while you're on another call.
  • Depends on: A phone app that reports being in another call.

calls.later_min

Minutes until it calls back after you tap Later on a ringing call.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 30.
  • When to change it: Set it to how long you're usually busy when you tap Later.

calls.review

After every call, an AI request looks back at it: does what slipped through and keeps lessons.

It finishes anything the call promised but didn't do, and keeps short lessons every later call is told.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to save AI requests, or if you don't want calls read back. Calls still work the same.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: The call's transcript goes to your AI (or calls.review_brain) once per call.

calls.review_brain

Which AI does the look back after a call; empty is your main one.

  • Values: An AI id such as agy, gemini or claude, or empty. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Pick a cheaper AI if look backs use too much of your main AI's limit.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: That AI gets the call's transcript.
  • Depends on: calls.review.

calls.review_effort

How hard the look back after a call thinks.

  • Values: One of: low; medium; high. Default: high.
  • When to change it: Lower it to spend less of your AI's limit on look backs.
  • Depends on: calls.review.

calls.lessons_max

How many lessons from past calls every call is told; a repeat replaces the older one.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 12.
  • When to change it: Lower it if calls feel weighed down by old rules; raise it if useful lessons drop off.
  • Depends on: calls.review.

calls.notes_repo

Your notes' repository on GitHub, so calls can read whole notes while this computer is off.

  • Values: owner/repo; set by Notes sync. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Set for you by Notes sync. Empty it if calls shouldn't read your notes from GitHub.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Your call line reads notes from that repository with Notes sync's token, only during a call.
  • Depends on: Notes sync, and notes_sync.share_with_calls.

calls.ntfy_server

The push server the phone app listens on. Change it only if you run your own.

  • Values: A web address. Default: https://ntfy.sh.
  • When to change it: Only if you host your own ntfy server. Pair the phone again afterwards.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Wake-up pushes (no content) go through this server.

calls.today

Your phone app's Today screen and its copy of your alarm, kept up to date from this computer.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't use the app's Today screen. The app then says it's turned off.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed for each phone with its own key through your call line, every 5 minutes when it changed.
  • Depends on: Phone calls and a paired Android phone.

calls.views

Systems and the Notes map in the phone app: your notes' titles and links, and each feature's health.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on to see your notes map on the phone. Off by default because it sends note titles to the phone.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Note titles and links (never what notes say), sealed with the phone's key, through your call line.
  • Depends on: Phone calls and a paired Android phone.

calls.views_notes

The most notes on the phone's Notes map (best linked first).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 5000.
  • When to change it: Lower it if the map is slow on your phone.
  • Depends on: calls.views.

calls.views_links

The most links on the phone's Notes map (a book's "Seen in" links go first).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 20000.
  • When to change it: Lower it if the map is slow or cluttered on your phone.
  • Depends on: calls.views.

calls.worker.url

Your call line's address (set when it's deployed).

  • Values: Text. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Never by hand: deploying the call line sets it.

calls.worker.name

Your call line's name on Cloudflare (set when it's deployed).

  • Values: Text. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Never by hand: deploying the call line sets it.

calls.worker.version

The version of your call line that's deployed (set by Kurooto).

  • Values: Text. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Never by hand: Kurooto updates the call line and writes it here.

calls.chat_sessions

Separate chats in the phone app, each its own conversation whose context carries over.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep one chat, as on Telegram.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Messages travel sealed through your call line; each chat's context goes to your AI with its requests.
  • Depends on: Phone calls and a paired phone.

calls.chat_files_inline

Files in the app's chat show inline and open in the app.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to get files as a link instead.
  • Depends on: Phone calls and a paired phone.

calls.chat_edit

Edit your own messages in the app's chat for 15 minutes after sending.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if edits confuse you; Kurooto isn't asked again after an edit either way.
  • Depends on: Phone calls and a paired phone.

calls.chat_titles

How a new chat gets its title: its first words, or a short title from your AI.

  • Values: first words or ai. Default: first words.
  • When to change it: Pick ai for neater titles; it costs one small AI request per new chat.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: ai sends the chat's first message and answer to your AI once.

calls.chat_desk

What you say at the desk (aloud, Live, the board's talk box) and its answers, as one Desk chat on the dashboard and phone.

Your own turns only, never a guest's. The Desk chat sits beside your other chats; its messages are sealed to your call line like the rest and come to the phone without a notification. A message you type in it follows on from what you said at the desk.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep desk talk out of the chat list (the Desk chat and its context are kept until it's back on).
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed copies on your call line, as for every chat message.
  • Depends on: calls.chat_sessions.

calls.chat_desk_history

The board's talk box sends the Desk chat's recent turns with each request, so your AI can follow on.

Only the dashboard's talk box: the kurooto command, the terminal tab and setup stay requests on their own.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you use the talk box for back-and-forth conversations.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Longer requests to your AI (the recent turns go with each one).
  • Depends on: calls.chat_desk.

lidsafe.hours

How far ahead your call line holds reminders and the morning line, in hours.

  • Values: 12 to 24. Default: 24.
  • When to change it: Lower it to keep less on the call line; 24 covers a whole night and day away.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Your call line keeps each item as a time, an id and ciphertext it can't read.
  • Depends on: Reminders with the lid shut, Phone calls and an Android phone.

lidsafe.reminders

Your reminders still reach the phone while this computer is asleep or off.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to have reminders only from this computer (late, if it slept).
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed for each Android phone with its own key; your call line holds ciphertext only.
  • Depends on: Reminders with the lid shut.

lidsafe.today

A morning line on your phone with the day's first class and what's due, even with the lid shut.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if the morning page and check-ins are enough.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed for each Android phone; your call line holds ciphertext only.
  • Depends on: Reminders with the lid shut.

lidsafe.morning

When the morning line comes if no wake-up alarm is set.

  • Values: A time of day, 24-hour HH:MM. Default: 07:30.
  • When to change it: Set it to when you're usually up. With the alarm set, it comes with the alarm.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed for each Android phone; your call line holds ciphertext only.
  • Depends on: lidsafe.today.

calls.views_brain

Show the Brain console in the phone app's Views (the Brain choice), sealed for your phone like Systems and the Notes map.

  • Values: true (the phone gets the Brain's copy, but only while you're looking at it), false (the Brain choice says it's off). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather the Brain's decisions and plans never travel to your phone.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: A sealed copy of what the Brain logged (decisions, plans, beliefs: never health or money) passes through your call line while you watch it.
  • Depends on: Phone views (calls.views) and Brain on, with the Brain console (mind.console.on).

calls.views_being

Show the being in the phone app's Views: the dashboard's own body of Kurooto, drawn on the phone from a sealed copy.

  • Values: true (each Systems copy carries the being's block: features on or off, AI requests and their titles, what's scheduled, the coil's readings, Voice's state, event kinds and times, the panels' settings), false (the Being choice says it's off). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather the titles of your AI requests and schedule never travel to your phone. Note previews are only fetched when you tap a note.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: A slightly bigger sealed copy through your call line every few seconds while Views is open.
  • Depends on: Phone views (calls.views).

calls.context_brief

Calls and desk Live talks hear what every AI request is told: your profile's brief, how to help you, topics you skip.

Only text your AI already gets with every request. Never your read, mood, tone or senses; the call's lessons go once, as before.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather calls knew only what's due and the persona. Takes effect on the next call or desk talk.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: That text goes to Google's Gemini Live with each call or desk talk, and to your call line in the offline brief.

calls.lessons_everywhere

Each lesson from the look back after a call is also a dated line in your how-to-help-me note, so every AI request follows it.

The lines go under "Learned on calls". A lesson replaced, dropped or forgotten with /lessons takes its line out too. The 90-day "Still true?" asks about them like your other kept lines.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather keep call lessons to calls. Lines already copied stay until you remove them.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: The lines go with every AI request to the AI you picked, as the rest of the note does.
  • Depends on: calls.review.

calls.tell_senses

A call's brief says whether Face presence and Voice ID are on, and when you were last at the desk.

Sense names, a word (at the desk, away) and a time only: never camera frames, scores or anyone else seen.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if calls needn't know whether you're at your computer.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Those few words go to Gemini Live with the call's brief.

calls.read_agent_tools

Calls and desk Live talks also get the read-only tools your AI has, like the concept graph's search and books' pages.

Only tools that only read (never one that writes, sends or needs your tap). Guests never get tools.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if calls feel slower to start: each tool makes the call's instructions longer.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: What those tools read goes to Gemini Live when the call uses them.
  • Depends on: The features that give the tools (concept graph, Books, Learns from its own work, Classroom).

calls.read_tools

One feature's read-only tools left off calls, e.g. {"books": false}.

  • Values: A JSON object, feature → false. Default: none.
  • When to change it: When one feature's tools aren't useful on calls but the others are.
  • Depends on: calls.read_agent_tools.

calls.today_waiting

The phone app's Today says how many messages are held, how many proposals wait, and whether the Brain is awake.

Counts and a word only. Tapping the held line sends today's held messages now.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather not see those on the phone.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed for each phone, with the rest of Today.
  • Depends on: calls.today.

calls.settle_messages

After a call or a desk talk went over something, its held messages and open questions are closed, not just marked.

Off, they're only marked "talked through on your 17:00 call" (with notify.calm.recheck) and the Waiting page has Settled for each. On, they're settled at once. Approvals never: they wait for your tap.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if a call usually covers what was waiting.

calls.desk_first

A call Kurooto places by itself is offered at your desk first when you're there alone; the phone still rings after a wait.

Never the alarm or a call you asked for just now. It asks aloud "shall we do it here?"; a yes takes it at the desk (Live if voice.listen.live is on, else Voice's own way), and its words go where the call's would (a check-in's journal). No or silence: the phone rings, as before.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you're often at the computer when check-in calls come.
  • Depends on: Voice speaking and listening, and Face presence seeing you at the desk.

calls.desk_first_s

How long the desk offer waits for your yes before the phone rings (seconds, at least 10).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 30.
  • When to change it: Raise it if you need longer to answer; lower it if the phone should ring sooner.

calls.today_plan

The phone app's Today also shows the first lines of today's plan, what to start, and the hours studied.

The plan's first lines from your daily note (with what a call changed since it was drafted), Start-by's and revision's rows.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if Today should stay as short as before.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed for each phone, with the rest of Today.
  • Depends on: calls.today.

calls.after_infer

After a call, what you said on it is read like a typed message: stated facts are set up, "remember that" is kept.

Your lines only, not Kurooto's. Each thing set up is told once with its Undo, as for typed messages.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if calls should only do what their tools did.
  • Depends on: Set up from what you say (infer.on) and Remember, for each part.

calls.threads_live

A call can close one of your open threads while it's on, when you say it's done.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if threads should close only after the call, from its transcript.
  • Depends on: memory.threads.

calls.mode_when_moving

A call Kurooto places by itself while your phone says you're walking or in a vehicle uses the phone's own speech.

It never holds or delays the call; calls you ask for and the alarm are unchanged. The motion reading stays on this computer.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if Live calls on the move keep breaking up.
  • Depends on: Brain's phone motion sense (mind.senses.phone.motion).

Morning alarm

Kurooto wakes you with a call that rings on the alarm stream (so it rings on silent), placed by your call line so it rings even if this computer is asleep. Set one in plain English ("wake me at 6:45"), with /alarm, or as a daily default here. While an alarm is set, the night runs from your bedtime to the alarm. More in Phone calls and the morning alarm.

For every setting in this group, unless its entry says otherwise:

  • Costs and what leaves this computer: The alarm is held by your call line (your own Cloudflare account) and rings your phone; the call itself is like any other (see Phone calls).
  • Depends on: The Morning alarm feature, with Phone calls and a paired phone.

alarm.daily

A wake-up time that rings every day (on the days below) unless you set another for that morning.

  • Values: A time HH:MM, or none (off). Default: none.
  • When to change it: Set it if you get up at the same time most days; "no alarm tomorrow" skips one day and it's back after. /alarm daily 7:00 and /alarm daily off change it too.

alarm.days

The days the daily alarm rings.

  • Values: A list of mon … sun. Default: ["mon", "tue", "wed", "thu", "fri", "sat", "sun"].
  • When to change it: Take the weekend out if you sleep in: ["mon", "tue", "wed", "thu", "fri"].
  • Depends on: alarm.daily.

alarm.reason

What the wake-up call says it's for.

  • Values: Short text. Default: Wake-up call.
  • When to change it: Change the words if "Wake-up call" isn't how you'd put it; it shows on the ringing screen.

alarm.retries

How many more times the alarm rings if you don't answer (5 minutes apart).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 2.
  • When to change it: Raise it if you sleep through it; 0 rings once.

alarm.snooze_min

Minutes a snoozed alarm waits before it rings again.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 10.
  • When to change it: Shorten it if you fall back asleep; Later on the ringing call uses it.

alarm.bedtime

With an alarm set, no calls from this time until it rings; none uses the night hours' start.

  • Values: A time HH:MM, or none. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Set it if you go to bed earlier than your night hours start on nights you've set an alarm.

alarm.prep_min

Minutes before the alarm that your AI drafts the day's plan into today's daily note.

  • Values: Minutes; 0: no plan. Default: 20.
  • When to change it: Set 0 if you don't want a drafted plan; raise it if the plan isn't ready when the alarm rings.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: One AI request each morning with an alarm; it reads your calendar, deadlines and notes for the day.
  • Depends on: Chat with your AI on.

alarm.notes

After the wake-up call, today's plan (or what you said on the call) goes into your daily note.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't want the morning call in your notes.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves: written to your notes on this computer.

alarm.open_page

When the alarm rings, the morning page opens on this computer (if it's awake).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if the page opening on a shared or sleeping-room screen bothers you.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves.

alarm.keep_awake

Keep this computer awake before the alarm so the day's plan is drafted in time.

From 12 hours before until 15 minutes after it rings. On Linux, with the lid closed too; a Mac still sleeps with its lid closed.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if the plan is often missing because the computer slept overnight. Plug it in: it stays awake all night.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Battery and power overnight.

alarm.suggest

Before your usual bedtime with no alarm set, one message offering your usual wake-up time.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't use an alarm every night. It never sets one by itself.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: At most one Telegram message a day.

alarm.skip_if_up

Up before the alarm: one question, skip the ring? It rings as set unless you tap Skip.

In the hour before the alarm, when Face presence sees you at the desk, or you used the dashboard, the phone app or talked to it in the last ten minutes. Once per alarm.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather the alarm just rings.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: At most one message per alarm.

alarm.sleep_aware

Up late before a free morning: one offer of a later ring; after a short night the wake-up call keeps it brief.

With less than six hours left before the alarm and you still up, a later time about 7.5 hours from now, at least an hour before your first class (needs Classes). It moves the alarm only if you tap it. The wake-up call is told "a short night" (Brain's sleep saw it), never when you slept.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't want late-night alarm offers.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: At most one message per alarm.

Reminders, deadlines and start-by dates

The deadline list and how it chases what's due (a message a day before, a call or urgent message six hours before, "Overdue?" after), where deadlines come from (your notes, Classroom, the calendar, the term note, photos), and start-by dates worked back from your free time. Start-by is local arithmetic: no AI. More in Reminders and deadlines.

For every setting in this group, unless its entry says otherwise:

  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves this computer beyond the messages and calls themselves (Telegram, the phone app, your call line).
  • Depends on: The Deadlines feature.

deadlines.ping_h

Hours before a deadline that its first nudge comes (one message, with Done / Not mine / Snooze).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 24.
  • When to change it: Raise it to hear about deadlines earlier; lower it if a day's notice is too early to act.

deadlines.urgent_h

Hours before a deadline that it's urgent: a call (with Phone calls) or an urgent message.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 6.
  • When to change it: Raise it if six hours isn't enough to finish things; lower it if the urgent nudge comes too early.

deadlines.call

The urgent nudge is a call, when Phone calls is on (a message when no call fits before it).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to get a message instead of a call.
  • Depends on: Phone calls.

deadlines.plan_h

How far ahead "due soon" looks, in hours: the morning plan, /status and calls.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 72.
  • When to change it: Raise it to see the week's deadlines every morning; lower it to see only the next day or two.

deadlines.overdue_check

After a deadline's due time: "Overdue?" with Done / New date / Drop.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather let past deadlines drop quietly.

deadlines.tick_notes

Done ticks the - [ ] line in your notes when the deadline came from one.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if Kurooto shouldn't change your task lines.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves: it changes only that checkbox.
  • Depends on: deadlines.scan_notes.

deadlines.scan_notes

Find deadlines in your notes: dated - [ ] tasks.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if your notes' dated tasks aren't deadlines you want chased.

deadlines.skip_notes

Notes or folders the deadline scan leaves alone.

  • Values: A list of note paths or folders inside your notes. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Add an ideas list or an archive whose dates aren't due dates.
  • Depends on: deadlines.scan_notes.

deadlines.max_files

The most notes read when looking for deadlines (notes over 1 MB are skipped).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 3000.
  • When to change it: Raise it if you have a very large notes folder and some tasks are missed; lower it on a slow computer.
  • Depends on: deadlines.scan_notes.

deadlines.from_calendar

Calendar events that look like deadlines or exams join the deadline list.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if your calendar's events aren't deadlines.
  • Depends on: The Calendar feature.

deadlines.from_term_note

The term note's quizzes, exams and deadlines join the deadline list.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you keep that list elsewhere.
  • Depends on: Subjects and timetable, with a term note (/term note).

deadlines.from_photos

Send a photo with the caption "dates" and its dates come back as buttons to add.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you never send notice-board photos.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: The photo goes to your AI to read its dates.

deadlines.recurring

Recurring quizzes (from kurooto/recurring.md in your notes) become dated deadlines.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't keep a recurring quizzes table.

deadlines.horizon_days

How far ahead recurring quizzes become dated deadlines, in days.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 30.
  • When to change it: Lower it to keep the list short; raise it to see a whole term ahead.
  • Depends on: deadlines.recurring.

deadlines.exam_mode_days

Days before a quiz or exam that exam mode starts (more practice, revision); 0 turns it off.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 14.
  • When to change it: Raise it if you start revising earlier; 0 if you don't want exam mode.
  • Depends on: Quiz for the practice it brings.

deadlines.meeting_brief

The evening before a meeting with a project's person: a short brief of where things stand.
