  • When to change it: Lower it to clear the door sooner; nothing is ever dropped, only moved to the bundle.
  • Depends on: mind.workspace.arbiter and Calm (notify.calm.on); with Calm off it simply goes.

mind.workspace.item_fatigue

Deadline, class and quiz reminders also tire at the door, and need their item to be on Brain's mind, like its promises.

These messages name their item for Brain's workspace (a deadline, a class or a quiz). By default that only makes one count double at the door while the item is on Brain's mind. On, it also counts toward the item's fatigue (told three times today, it drops out) and, with the door off, the gate holds one whose item didn't win.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if reminders about the same thing come too often; more of them then wait for the evening bundle.
  • Depends on: mind.workspace.on.

mind.expect.on

Each message Kurooto starts says what it expects (a reply, or done within 2 hours), and what came of it is recorded.

A question or check-in expects a reply within 3 hours; a nudge expects to be acted on within 2. Your reply, a tap on one of its buttons or the time running out closes it; the scorekeeper writes the outcome. Ids and numbers only, never the words.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to stop recording expectations and outcomes.

mind.why.on

"Why this?" under a message Kurooto started says what Brain's gate and door did with it, what it expected, and how many it has sent today.

It adds to Calm's Why? on Telegram, and puts a Why this? button on the phone app chat's copy.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep Calm's Why? as it was.

mind.why.to_ai

A message that asks why also gives your AI the plain reasons for Kurooto's own last few messages.

For a message with the word why: the last three messages Kurooto started by itself in the last day, each with its time, the plain reason, the setting, whether Brain's gate made it quieter, and what it expected and what came of it. Never a sense, the read, a mood or a tone, and not the door's own words. "Why did you greet me?" then gets the real reason.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't want those reasons in requests to your AI.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: A few lines more in requests that ask why.
  • Depends on: Brain and mind.why.on.

mind.why.console_link

Why? ends with a link that opens that message's decision in the Brain console.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to leave the link out of Why?.
  • Depends on: mind.why.on; the link opens the dashboard on this computer.

mind.attention.on

A model of its own attention: "what are you thinking about?" is answered from it.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't ask.
  • Depends on: mind.workspace.on.

mind.beliefs.on

Keep where each belief came from (said, saw, inferred, imagined) and how sure it is.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to drop the hedging rules. /beliefs lists them.

mind.tools.on

Your AI and calls can read Brain's plan, when you're likely free (in words) and your precedents; "call me when I'm free" works.

Three read-only tools: brain_plan (as /plan), brain_free_time ("Likely free from 16:00", never a chance), brain_precedents (as /precedents). schedule_call takes when "when I'm free" and books the forecast's hour, still asking for your Approve outside a call.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep Brain's plan and forecast away from your AI.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Those lines go to the AI you picked when it asks.
  • Depends on: Brain; the free-time part needs mind.presence_forecast.on.

mind.tools.feelings

Your AI, calls and desk Live can ask how Kurooto feels and why: its own feeling, what it's about, its plan and its top concerns.

The read-only tool brain_feelings (0.9.3): its own feeling in words ("uneasy about something I said I'd do: remind you to book the lab slot"), the promise behind it and whether it's since been kept, when it rechecks, and its top concerns ("Organic Chemistry: Quiz (due in 22 hours)"). Never its read of you, your mood or tone, its forecast of you, health or money, and no scores.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep Brain's feelings out of your AI's answers; it then only gets the one-line headline it always had.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Those lines go to the AI you picked when it asks (on a call or Live, to Google).
  • Depends on: Brain, mind.tools.on and mind.feelings.on.

mind.precedents.on

Judgement calls between your values are asked once, then followed (off until you turn it on).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you'd like fewer repeat questions. It can never unlock anything on the always-ask list.

mind.precedents.threshold

How alike two situations must be (0 to 1) for a precedent to be followed.

  • Values: A number. Default: 0.75.
  • When to change it: Raise it to be asked more often; lower it to follow precedents more widely.
  • Depends on: mind.precedents.on.

mind.play.on

A little nightly time to explore its own questions on your topics (off until you turn it on).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you're curious what it finds. Outputs are never shown unprompted.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Runs on this computer, on the charger with the lid open and the computer cool; its outputs stay in Kurooto's state folder.

mind.play.minutes_per_night

Minutes of play a night, on the charger only.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 20.
  • When to change it: Lower it to save the battery and your AI.
  • Depends on: mind.play.on.

mind.forget.on

Old things fade from use, old misses stop coming up, and /forget goes everywhere Brain kept something.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Leave it on; off, faded items rank as fresh and old misses can come up again.

mind.forget.half_lives.default

Days for a remembered item to fade to half, when its kind has no own setting.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 60.
  • When to change it: Raise it to keep things in use longer.
  • Depends on: mind.forget.on.

mind.forget.half_lives.fact

Days for a fact about you to fade to half.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 90.
  • When to change it: Raise it if facts fade too soon.
  • Depends on: mind.forget.on.

mind.forget.half_lives.gist

Days for a conversation's gist to fade to half.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 30.
  • When to change it: Raise it to keep older conversations in use.
  • Depends on: mind.forget.on.

mind.forget.half_lives.project

Days for a project fact to fade to half.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 180.
  • When to change it: Raise it for long projects.
  • Depends on: mind.forget.on.

mind.forget.misses_weeks

After this many weeks, a past miss is no longer brought up.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 6.
  • When to change it: Lower it to be reminded of misses for less time.
  • Depends on: mind.forget.on.

mind.plans.on

Plans over weeks: which milestones are at risk, from simulated futures. A suggestion, never a push.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't use /plan.

mind.plans.rollouts

How many simulated futures a plan runs.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 1000.
  • When to change it: Lower it on a slow computer (rougher odds).
  • Depends on: mind.plans.on.

mind.plans.weeks

How far ahead (weeks) the simulated futures look.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 6.
  • When to change it: Raise it for long projects.
  • Depends on: mind.plans.on.

mind.tom.on

A model of what you know, believe, want and prefer, each row with its source (/model).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't want one kept. It never models health, relationships, politics, religion or money.

mind.tom.false_beliefs

When something you were told turns out wrong, tell you once, with the evidence.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather not get these corrections.
  • Depends on: mind.tom.on.

mind.tom.from_chat

Goals, due dates and exam results you mention in your messages go into Brain's model of you.

"My goal is to finish the draft by Friday" becomes a stated goal; "the essay is due Monday" (one of your open deadlines) becomes what you believe about its date, so a wrong date can be pointed out once. An exam result counts as evidence of what you know: it adds a dated line in Kurooto's own block of the course's exam prep note, and after a low score offers more preparation hours (a tap). Never health, money, passwords or the deny-list's topics. Kept on this computer.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep Brain's model to your deadlines, projects and what Kurooto told you, and results you mention from changing anything.
  • Depends on: mind.tom.on; Subjects for exam results.

mind.tom.attention

Hold a message (at most 15 minutes) while you're deep in one project.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if interruptions while you're focused bother you.
  • Depends on: mind.tom.on and the activity stream (mind.senses.activity).

mind.senses.intero

Its own machine's state every minute: temperatures, memory, battery, disk, AI limits.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to stop Brain sensing the computer.

mind.senses.activity

The activity stream: the app in front, hashed window titles, saves and commits (never keystrokes, the screen or URLs).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on for plans and skills that learn from what you do. It's never used to check up on you.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves. Raw events last 7 days, then only totals.

mind.senses.phone

Each of your phone's sensors, off until you switch it on (here and in the phone app).

  • Values: A JSON object, sensor → on/off: screen, charging, motion, steps, light, places, headphones, dnd, alarm, wifi, usage. Default: every sensor off.
  • When to change it: Switch on the ones you're happy to share; both here and on the phone must be on.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Readings are sealed on the phone and travel through your call line, which can't read them.
  • Depends on: A paired Android phone with the sensor on in the app.

mind.senses.places_to_addons

A fresh arrival at or leaving of a place you saved on the phone also reaches add-ons that ask for it.

Add-ons with their own use for it (dimming the room's light, say) get the place's name, its arrive or leave and the time, on this computer only. Each add-on's own switch decides whether it does anything; nothing changes until one does. The log notes the event without the name.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep your places to Kurooto's own features.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves this computer; the name never goes into an AI's context.
  • Depends on: Brain's phone places sensor (mind.senses.phone) and an add-on that uses it.

mind.skills.on

Solved tasks become tested, reusable skills; each new one starts out asking first.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather not have Brain learn skills. /learned lists them.

mind.skills.from_demos

Also offer skills from what you do over and over (asked once).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on with the activity stream if you'd like Brain to spot repeats.
  • Depends on: mind.skills.on and mind.senses.activity.

mind.finetune.on

A weekly local fine-tune of the model on this computer, from checked examples only.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on only with an 8 GB graphics card and the training packages.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: A night of graphics-card time on the charger; nothing leaves.

mind.finetune.day

The night of the week it trains.

  • Values: One of: mon; tue; wed; thu; fri; sat; sun. Default: sun.
  • When to change it: Pick a night the computer is plugged in.
  • Depends on: mind.finetune.on.

mind.finetune.base

The local model's name in Ollama to build the adapter into; empty is the helper model.

  • Values: Text. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Only if you want another base model.
  • Depends on: mind.finetune.on.

mind.finetune.base_hf

The Hugging Face name of the base model, which the trainer loads.

  • Values: Text. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Set it when you set mind.finetune.base.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: The base weights are downloaded from Hugging Face once.
  • Depends on: mind.finetune.on.

mind.finetune.min_examples

With fewer checked examples than this, it skips the week.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 30.
  • When to change it: Raise it for steadier adapters.
  • Depends on: mind.finetune.on.

mind.finetune.practice

Include the harness' practice tasks in training.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to train only on your own checked work.
  • Depends on: mind.finetune.on.

mind.finetune.probe_words

Words of a forgotten prompt kept to check a new adapter forgot it (3 or more).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 5.
  • When to change it: Leave it unless the check is too strict or too loose.
  • Depends on: mind.finetune.on.

mind.harness.on

Nightly on the charger: how it does on tasks it has never seen, and checks of Brain's own promises.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Leave it on: without it, nothing can be promoted. /capabilities shows the scores.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Runs on this computer with its own rules (no AI requests), on the charger.

mind.harness.rotate_days

The hidden tasks are made new this often (days), so old ones can't be memorised.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 30.
  • When to change it: Leave it.
  • Depends on: mind.harness.on.

mind.improve.on

Brain improving itself through gated proposals: nothing changes without the hidden harness and your say-so.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to stop it proposing changes to itself. /improve lists them.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Challengers run on this computer; applying one is always your tap.
  • Depends on: mind.harness.on.

mind.lab.on

Overnight pre-registered experiments on the projects you list (Linux and macOS, in a sandbox).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't use the lab. Your files are never changed; winners come as proposals.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Runs on this computer, on the charger, with no network.

mind.lab.projects

Folders (or repository projects' names) the lab may run experiments on.

  • Values: A list; /lab add <folder> adds one. Default: none.

mind.lab.max_per_night

mind.lab.run_wall_s

Seconds one lab run may take before it's stopped.

mind.lab.gpu_hours_per_night

mind.lab.seed

A fixed seed for the sampled configurations; 0 draws a new sample each day.

mind.lab.in_repo

Keep a project's lab protocol in its .kurooto folder (off: in Kurooto's own state).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.

mind.heartbeat.on

When this computer is down, your call line also says what the next 12 hours have no guarantee for.

mind.promises.on

Its own promises ("I'll check back at 6") are noticed, kept, or owned up to first.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.

mind.promises.answers

Promises in its answers to you, its side of a call and what it says at the desk are tracked too, not only in messages it starts.

Read on this computer with the same rules (an "I'll" with a time); nothing is sent anywhere. A broken one is owned up to like the rest.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to track only the messages it starts by itself.

mind.story.on

One life story every part of Kurooto reads first: a fixed core, then its recent weeks.

mind.story.tokens

The life story's summary is at most this many tokens (about 4 characters each).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 800.
  • When to change it: Lower it to send less with each request.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: That many tokens go with each AI request.

mind.backfill.on

Brain reads what Kurooto kept before it was on, once (/mind backfill); off until you turn it on.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on so Brain's read, forecasts and plans start with your past. /mind backfill forget undoes it.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.

mind.console.on

The Brain console: a dashboard page (Alt+O) and the phone's Brain view showing what the Brain decided and why.

  • Values: true (the page and the view read what the Brain keeps), false (both say the console is off; the Brain itself runs as before). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't want its reasoning on screen. It only reads; nothing the Brain does depends on it.
  • Depends on: Brain on.

mind.console.edit

Wrong and Forget beside each row of the model of you on the dashboard's Brain console (the phone's view stays read-only).

The same as /model wrong and /model forget, only on your click. A Wrong on a line Kurooto wrote in your how-to-help-me note offers to take the line out of the note as well, which happens only when you confirm; your own lines are never offered.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep the console read-only.
  • Depends on: mind.console.on.

mind.dream.on

Brain goes over the day at night and brings cards, how-to-help lines and a voice line to the morning message.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to stop all of its dreaming at once; each piece also has its own switch.

mind.dream.cards

Each night, 1 to 3 quiz cards for concepts you studied that day that are on your frontier, kept by a tap.

Concepts you changed notes on, answered quiz cards about or logged study time for, that Brain's model of what you know puts on your frontier (not mastered, prerequisites solid). The model on this computer writes each card from your note; without one it's a plain "what is ...?" card. They wait in the morning message with Keep and Drop; a kept card goes into Quiz, first due about a day after it was written. /mind shows your recall on them next to your own cards.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather write every card yourself.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves: the local model or a fixed card. A card you keep is graded like any other (by your AI with Chat on).
  • Depends on: mind.tom.on and Quiz.

mind.dream.cards_max

The most dream cards a night (1 to 3).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 3.
  • When to change it: Lower it for fewer cards to look at in the morning.
  • Depends on: mind.dream.cards.

mind.dream.lessons

Each night, 1 to 3 lines for your how-to-help-me note from the day's outcomes, written only when you tap Keep.

It replays what you undid, the messages left unanswered or given Less of this, the times you told Voice off and its guesses about you marked wrong; clear patterns become lines like "Fewer nudges in the evening". Keep writes the line (with Undo); Not that drops it for good.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather write that note yourself.

mind.dream.voice

One morning line on what the nightly voice learning saw: turns it learned from and any misfire pattern.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't need it. Counts and flags only, never words.
  • Depends on: Voice with voice.listen.learn_nightly.

mind.dream.from_wrong

The nightly lessons also learn from your Wrong taps: two on the same kind of request suggest a how-to-help-me line.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to leave Wrong out of the lessons. A line is only written when you tap Keep.
  • Depends on: mind.dream.lessons and Wrong button and test cases.

mind.dream.from_calls

The nightly lessons also count what the look back after calls flagged (counts only, never a call's words).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to leave calls out of the lessons.
  • Depends on: mind.dream.lessons and Phone calls' look back (calls.review).

mind.dream.from_proposals

The nightly lessons also count proposals you discarded: two from the same source suggest fewer of them.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to leave proposals out of the lessons.
  • Depends on: mind.dream.lessons.

mind.study_push.on

One morning line about the subject Brain is most uneasy about (due date, minutes logged, cards due), with Yes and Later.

That day the subject's day-before deadline reminder, Start-by's "Time to start" and exam mode's daily practice check-in are folded into it. Yes sets a reminder at the time it offers (or counts the deadline as started). The six-hour reminder, "Overdue?" and the evening-before practice offer still come.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to get the separate reminders back.
  • Depends on: Deadlines (a deadline with a subject); Study and Quiz add their numbers when on.

mind.study_push.when

When the morning line comes.

  • Values: A schedule like daily 07:55. Default: daily 07:55.
  • When to change it: Keep it before Start-by's morning pass (startby.when), so its message is folded.
  • Depends on: mind.study_push.on.

mind.study_push.minutes

How long a study stretch the morning line offers.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 25.
  • When to change it: Change it to the length you'd actually start with.
  • Depends on: mind.study_push.on.

mind.study_push.frontier

Between subjects it's equally uneasy about, the one with concepts you're ready for wins, and the line names one.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you'd like the morning line to say where to go next in the subject.
  • Depends on: mind.study_push.on and Brain's model of what you know (mind.tom.on).

mind.study_push.horizon_days

How far ahead a deadline can be for Brain to be uneasy about it.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 7.
  • When to change it: Lower it to hear only about what's close.
  • Depends on: mind.study_push.on.

mind.study_push.start_timer

The reminder a Yes sets carries Start 25 min (Study mode's timer for that deadline), and Study mode opens on that subject.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep the reminder plain. The timer starts only on your tap.
  • Depends on: Brain's study push and the Study tracker.

mind.checkins.surprise

The next check-in comes early when the day departs strongly from Brain's forecast.

Two hours past the predicted wake time with no sign of you while your phone shows you up, or a deadline moved twice in a day: the next check-in planned today comes now instead of at its time, once a day. Never in a class or while you're asleep.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to have check-ins only at their times.
  • Depends on: mind.forecast.on and Check-ins (the phone's screen sense for the wake case).

mind.status.on

One status stream: focus, asleep, messages held, the one thing it's uneasy about, its own mood and arousal.

Enums and numbers only, made again whenever something happens (at most once a second) and pushed to the dashboard's being, copied to the phone's Views, and handed to add-ons that listen (an add-on's own face, for example). The mood is Brain's own; nothing in it says how you are. It stays on this computer and your phone's sealed copy.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off and the being, the phone and add-ons stop getting it: the glance and the mood in the drawing go neutral.
  • Depends on: Brain on.

mind.knobs.on

Three knobs: its mood sets how much it explores, arousal how soon it speaks up, calibration whether it asks first.

Mood: with a low mood the lab and play get half their nightly budget (never more than you set), and an add-on's interest bar rises a little. Arousal: how soon unprompted things may go (the arbiter's longest hold is scaled by 0.75 to 1.25). Calibration: while its beliefs score no better than a coin flip (a Brier score of 0.25 or worse, after 10), its own acts above R0 ask first. Drawn as the being's pupil and pulse. Nothing here touches what you asked for: no confirm step, no slower answer. Add-ons read it with kurooto.mind.status.knobs(app).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep the budgets and asking as they were in 0.8.13.
  • Depends on: Brain on.

mind.register.on

One temperament: its mood and arousal set how long its unprompted words are, its call openers and Voice's pace.

Quiet (low mood), even or lively (high mood and roused). An AI request Kurooto starts itself is told at most 2, 3 or 4 sentences for anything that goes to you unasked; a call it places itself opens in one short sentence, a greeting and the reason, or two warm sentences; what Voice says unasked (a greeting, an add-on's line) is spoken at voice.speak.rate scaled within 8% either way. Never for replies to you or calls you asked for.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep every unprompted message, opener and pace as in 0.8.13.
  • Depends on: Brain on.

mind.context.addons

Let an add-on add a few short lines of its own to your AI requests, when its own switch allows it.

At most four lines of 300 characters, from all add-ons together, after Brain's hints. Each add-on decides by its own switch whether its lines may reach an AI at all (one that keeps what it saw on this computer starts with that off). Never in a guest's request.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep every add-on's lines out of your requests.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: What the add-on's lines say goes with each request to the AI you picked.
  • Depends on: An add-on that offers lines, with its own switch on.

mind.context.on

Every AI request gets Brain's hints: concepts you're shaky on, and what's on its mind.

Read from Brain's own files with no model call (under 50 ms): up to three concepts below solid from your quizzes and notes in the last two weeks, and the workspace's winners that aren't about you. Your read, tone, mood and body state never go in, so a cloud AI never gets them. Answers use it silently. Off, the workspace's winners go as they did in 0.8.13.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather requests carry nothing from Brain beyond its story and feelings.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: The concept names and what's on its mind go with each request to the AI you picked.
  • Depends on: Brain on; the hints need the model of you (mind.tom.on).

mind.act.more

Its trust decides study blocks, reshaped plans, revision packs and keeping its night's cards and lines.

Five kinds of thing it proposes: a study block from the morning line (R1), moving part of a slipping deadline's work to tomorrow (R2), a revision pack the evening before a quiz or exam (R1, with packs set to ask), keeping a quiz card it wrote at night (R1) and keeping a how-to-help-me line from the night (R2). While its trust in that class is below the bar (mind.autonomy.thresholds), it asks with the feature's own buttons, as before, and each yes is an OK that builds trust. Once it's above, it does it and says so with Undo; an Undo costs trust and pauses the class for a week. A low mood, or beliefs no better than a guess lately (mind.knobs.on), make it ask first again. It works whether or not mind.autonomy.enforce is on, and needs no shadow weeks: the bar is the gate. /mind and /autonomy list each kind with its trust.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to have every one of them ask, as in 0.8.15.
  • Depends on: mind.autonomy.on; each kind needs its feature (Deadlines, Start-by, Reminders, Skills, Quiz, Brain's dreaming).

mind.act.record_asked

The Sunday list also shows what tools changed because you asked on a call or in a chat, with Undo.

Never an order or anything about money. It doesn't change what needs your OK: these ran because you asked.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to list only what it did on its own.
  • Depends on: mind.autonomy.on.

mind.trust.from_asked_undo

An Undo of a change you asked for also lowers its trust in that kind of action.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Leave it off: nothing it did on its own happened there. Turn it on if you'd rather every Undo make it more careful.
  • Depends on: mind.act.record_asked.

mind.presence_forecast.on

Forecast, hour by hour for the next six hours, when you'll be at your desk and free; scored against what the senses then show.

Free means at your desk (the activity sense saw the computer in use, or Face presence saw you, in the last quarter hour), not on a call and not in a class; the night and Kurooto's sleep window count as not free. It learns each hour of the week from its looks every 15 minutes. Each hour that's over is scored against the forecast made hours before and against "same as yesterday"; Brain is paid a little for beating that baseline (at most 0.05 a day of its reward), for accuracy only: nothing here counts what you said to Kurooto. /mind shows its accuracy and the next likely-free hour. Numbers only, on this computer.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't use the activity sense or Face presence (without them nothing is scored), or don't want it.
  • Depends on: Brain on; the activity sense (mind.senses.activity) or Face presence to score it.

mind.presence_forecast.prefer

Check-ins and calls Kurooto places by itself prefer an hour you're likely free.

A check-in just planned for an hour the forecast thinks you won't be free (a class, a call, an hour you're usually away) moves to a clearly likelier one nearby, the same check-in day; the night check-in isn't held to your sleep. A call Kurooto places by itself (not one you asked for, not an alarm, not a deadline under 12 hours away) waits for a likelier time when you aren't seen at your desk, and says so. With nothing learned yet, only classes, the night and calls move anything.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep check-ins and calls exactly at their times.
  • Depends on: mind.presence_forecast.on.

mind.presence_forecast.within_min

How far (minutes) a check-in or a call may move to a likelier hour.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 60.
  • When to change it: Lower it to keep them close to their times; 0 never moves them.
  • Depends on: mind.presence_forecast.prefer.

mind.presence_forecast.plan

Start-by and the study push use the hours you're likeliest free first; no hour is taken away.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on once the forecast has seen a few weeks. With few messages, it can also move Start-by's window to your usual hours, with Undo.
  • Depends on: Brain's presence forecast and Start-by dates.

mind.register.templates

Its ready-made unprompted messages keep the register's 2, 3 or 4 sentences too, not only what the AI writes.

A nudge or a line a feature sends by itself keeps its first sentences, and any later one its buttons answer (a question, or one naming a button). Digests and lists (more than one paragraph, or a line that starts a list) stay whole, and so do answers to you and anything exempt: alarms, your own reminders, a deadline under 12 hours away, calls, approvals, anything urgent.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to have Kurooto's ready-made messages come whole, as in 0.8.15.
  • Depends on: mind.register.on.

mind.tone.calls

With tone on, what you say on a call is tagged too, by the model on this computer.

Only your lines; the words are never kept, and no tone reaches an AI.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if only typed messages should count toward the day's tone.
  • Depends on: mind.tone.on.

mind.read.snoozes

The read counts how often you snoozed the wake-up alarm (Later on its call).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if snoozes shouldn't count toward how your days read.
  • Depends on: mind.read.on.

mind.read.skipped_classes

The read counts the hours of classes you said you'd skip that day.

Only from your own /skip or a call's skip_class, never guessed from where you are.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if skipped classes shouldn't count toward how your days read.
  • Depends on: mind.read.on and Subjects.

presence.owner_here.face

Face presence seeing your face counts as you being here (Brain's sense of your day, the forecast).

One words-free sign, owner_here (core/here.py), with the via only: your first and last sign of life of the day, and at your desk for the presence forecast. At most one event every 5 minutes; the time is kept in memory.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep your face out of Brain's sense of your day (Face presence itself is unchanged).
  • Depends on: Face presence on and enrolled.

presence.owner_here.voice

Voice ID calling a voice yours, even talk not to Kurooto, counts as you at the desk.

It also ends Face presence's "you've stepped away" (never as an arrival: no greeting), so talking from out of the camera's view isn't being away. Only the fact goes anywhere: never a word or the voice.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if talking near the computer shouldn't count as being at it.
  • Depends on: Voice listening with Voice ID on and enrolled.

presence.owner_here.dashboard

Using the dashboard (buttons, Ctrl+K, journal, capture, Study mode) counts as you at the desk.

Your first and last sign of life of the day, at your desk for the forecast, it ends Face presence's away (no greeting), and while Kurooto sleeps it wakes it, as a tap does.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if the dashboard is used by someone else, or from elsewhere.

presence.owner_here.phone

Using the phone app (chat, buttons, answers, Today) counts as you being around, not at the desk.

Your first and last sign of life of the day, and while Kurooto sleeps it wakes it, as a tap does. It never counts as at the desk. These fire with every phone sense off.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep phone app use out of Brain's sense of your day.
  • Depends on: Brain on (the phone signs are read through it).

mind.sleep.follow_desk

Starting a talk at the desk wakes Kurooto, and you at the desk near the night's end is you being up.

A look that opens a conversation, talking over it with a question or request, or a Gemini Live turn wakes it as its name does. Face presence seeing you at the desk in the night's last 90 minutes, while it's awake or woken, ends its night, and what waited for the morning goes out. Asleep, the latch holds: sitting down doesn't wake it.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to have only its name, /wake, a message, a tap or a call wake it, and only the alarm, "I'm up" or your phone end its night.
  • Depends on: mind.sleep.on; Voice listening and Face presence for the parts that need them.

mind.sleep.follow_external

An add-on's reading that you're asleep (a dark, quiet room, you away) starts Kurooto's night there.

An add-on with its own senses (the Body add-on's desk phone) can say you're asleep. The reading is always noted; with this on, the night starts at it, up to two hours early, as with your phone's onset. A window set by hand isn't moved.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if the add-on's room reading is a good sign you've gone to bed.
  • Depends on: mind.sleep.on and an add-on that reports it.

mind.sleep.follow_bedside

Opening bedside mode on your phone starts Kurooto's night there; closing it is you being up.

The same as an add-on's reading (mind.sleep.follow_external): up to two hours before the usual start, never later. A window set by hand isn't moved. Only noted while this is on.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you open bedside mode when you go to bed.
  • Depends on: mind.sleep.on, a paired Android phone and phone.bedside.signal.

mind.presence_forecast.reachable

Check-ins and calls Kurooto places by itself go by whether you're reachable: at the desk or on your phone.

A second estimate beside at-the-desk, learned the same way from the phone app's use (and the phone's screen sense, if on). It's never lower than the desk one, and equals it until an hour of the week has been seen; it isn't scored. A call rings now while you're on your phone. What's said aloud keeps the desk estimate.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to have check-ins and calls go by the desk estimate alone, as in 0.8.18.
  • Depends on: mind.presence_forecast.prefer.

mind.presence_forecast.busy_hint

An add-on's usual busy hours (times only) show as a hint on /mind and Start-by messages.

For example "Tue and Thu evenings are usually busy." Nothing shows until an add-on gives busy times; never who or why.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to hide the hint.

mind.presence_forecast.visitors

An add-on's usual busy hours also lower the forecast there a little (never a block).

The chance you're free in those hours is cut by 40%, so a check-in or a call Kurooto places by itself may move to a nearby likelier hour. Times only, never who; nothing goes into a log or to an AI.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if those evenings really are bad times for Kurooto's own check-ins and calls.
  • Depends on: mind.presence_forecast.on, and an add-on that gives busy times.

Voice and voice notes

Kurooto speaks through this computer's speakers with a voice made here, and, if you turn listening on, hears the room through the microphone, all on this computer: noise and people talking to each other are dropped on the spot, and only what's said to Kurooto goes on, like a chat message. Voice notes you send on Telegram are written out by Gemini. More in Voice: talk and listen.

For every setting in this group, unless its entry says otherwise:

  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sound is handled on this computer and never touches the disk. Only turns said to Kurooto go to your AI, as a chat message would.
  • Depends on: The Voice feature.

voice.answers_questions

After the greeting's "Want to go through them?", a yes said aloud reads the waiting questions one by one and takes your spoken answers.

"skip" or "later" leaves one, "stop" ends it. Approvals are only counted; they still need your tap on the phone or Telegram. Only your voice counts (Voice ID), and nothing is read while someone else is here.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to have a yes go to your AI as before.
  • Depends on: Voice listening and Face presence's greeting with face.greet.talk.

voice.speak.on

Kurooto speaks through this computer's speakers.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep Voice for listening only, or for a silent room.

voice.speak.engine

The voice made on this computer: Kokoro (natural, about 0.55 GB), Piper (lighter) or off.

  • Values: kokoro, piper or off. Default: kokoro.
  • When to change it: Pick piper on an older computer; kokoro sounds more natural.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: The voice's files are downloaded once.
  • Depends on: voice.speak.on.

voice.speak.voice

Which Kokoro voice it speaks in; empty is af_heart.

  • Values: A Kokoro voice name: af_heart, af_bella, am_michael, bf_emma, bm_george and more. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Try a few with /voice say and keep the one you like.
  • Depends on: voice.speak.engine = kokoro.

voice.speak.rate

How fast it speaks: 1.0 is normal, 0.8 slower, 1.2 faster.

  • Values: A number around 0.5 to 2. Default: 1.0.
  • When to change it: Slow it down for long answers, speed it up if you're used to it.
  • Depends on: voice.speak.on.

voice.speak.what

What's read aloud: answers to what you said aloud, every AI answer, or nothing.

  • Values: voice (answers to what you said aloud), all (every AI answer) or off. Default: voice.
  • When to change it: Pick all if you work away from the screen.
  • Depends on: voice.speak.on.

voice.speak.max_chars

How much of a long answer is read aloud before it says "the rest is on screen".

  • Values: Characters. Default: 800.
  • When to change it: Raise it to hear long answers in full.
  • Depends on: voice.speak.on.

voice.speak.join_sentences

Speak short sentences together, up to about 200 characters at a time, instead of each on its own.

Kokoro reads a few words on their own with a hurried, flat tune; joined to their neighbours they get a natural one, and it still pauses at each full stop. The first piece is kept under about 120 characters, so speech starts as soon as before.

  • Values: true (short sentences joined) or false (one sentence at a time, as before 0.8.13). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if a long pause before the first word bothers you on a slow computer.
  • Depends on: voice.speak.on.

voice.speak.numbers_as_words

Say numbers as a person would: 21:31 as "nine thirty-one PM", ₹3,000 as "three thousand rupees".

Times, dates, years, money, versions, ranges, percentages and units are said in words: 12 GB as "twelve gigabytes", 0.8.13 as "zero point eight point thirteen", 2026 as "twenty twenty-six". Only what's said changes; the screen shows what was written.

  • Values: true (times, dates, years, money, versions, ranges and units in words) or false (the speech engine reads the digits itself). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if it ever reads a number wrongly and you'd rather hear the engine's own reading.
  • Depends on: voice.speak.on.

voice.listen.on

Listen through the microphone, all on this computer; only what's said to Kurooto leaves it.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on to talk to Kurooto out loud. It stops during calls, when paused, on battery saver and when muted.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: The speech models are downloaded once. Only turns addressed to Kurooto go to your AI.

voice.listen.wake_word

A wake-word model of your own (an openWakeWord .onnx path); empty: its name wakes it.

  • Values: A full path, or empty. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Only if you trained your own wake word.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice.listen.wake_threshold

How sure the wake-word model must be (0 to 1).

  • Values: A number. Default: 0.5.
  • When to change it: Raise it if it wakes by mistake; lower it if it misses you.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.wake_word.

voice.listen.name_variants

More spellings of its name to accept (the common mishearings of Kurooto are built in).

  • Values: A list of spellings. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Add the way the transcriber writes the name you gave it, if it keeps missing it.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice.listen.model_judge

When the rules can't tell whether you're talking to it, the model on this computer judges.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to use rules only (fewer false starts, more misses).
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves: a local model.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on and Local AI models.

voice.listen.threshold

How sure the local model must be (0 to 1) that speech is said to Kurooto.

  • Values: A number. Default: 0.7.
  • When to change it: Raise it if it answers conversations not meant for it; lower it if it ignores you.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.model_judge.

voice.listen.after_speak_s

Seconds after it spoke during which a question counts as an answer to it.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 6.
  • When to change it: Raise it if you think before answering.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice.listen.close_s

Seconds of nothing said to it before the spoken conversation closes.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 8.
  • When to change it: Raise it for slower back-and-forth.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice.listen.night

Keep listening during the night hours.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to stop listening at night.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice.listen.calls

Keep listening while you're on a phone call (off: listening pauses during calls).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on only if your calls are on another device out of earshot.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice.listen.phone_call

Stop listening while the phone app says it's in another call (WhatsApp, Meet), not only during Kurooto's own calls.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on so the desk stays quiet while you're on a call on the phone.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on and a phone app that reports being in another call.

voice.listen.stt_model

How big the speech-to-text model is: tiny (75 MB), base (145 MB) or small (484 MB, more accurate).

  • Values: tiny, base or small. Default: base.
  • When to change it: Pick small if it often mishears you and the computer is fast enough.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: The model is downloaded once.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice.listen.language

The language you speak, e.g. en or hi; empty works it out each time.

  • Values: A language code, or empty. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Set it if it guesses your language wrong, or misses its name: worked out on each short clip, an accent is often taken for another language (English for Hindi or Urdu) and the name never comes out right.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice.listen.early

Write the words out at your first short pause, so the turn goes on the moment you've finished.

At your first pause of about 0.3 seconds, what you've said so far is written out (on this computer) while Kurooto still waits to see if you go on. If you don't, the turn goes straight on with those words instead of writing the whole stretch out again, and a finished question (or a finished sentence of three words or more) ends the turn after 0.35 to 0.55 seconds of quiet instead of 0.7. If you go on talking, the early words are thrown away. With Voice ID's gate on, a voice that isn't yours (with no conversation open) is never written out early. Nothing is sent anywhere early: only the writing out happens sooner.

  • Values: true (default), false (the words are written out after 0.7 seconds of quiet, as before). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if Kurooto starts answering while you're only pausing mid-thought.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice.listen.auto_gain

Hearing follows the room: speech must stand above its noise, and a quiet voice is evened up.

While nothing is being said, Kurooto follows the room's noise floor (the loudness of what isn't speech, over a few seconds). Speech starts a stretch only when it's at least voice.listen.auto_gain_snr_db above that floor, so a noisy room asks for a clearer voice and a quiet one still hears a soft one. Each stretch is then brought to one level (never turned down, at most eight times louder, never clipped) before it's written out, so a quiet or far voice reads as well as a near one. All inside Kurooto: the system's microphone volume is never changed, so calls and other apps hear exactly as before. Only the noise floor and the last gain are kept (in memory, numbers only; /voice status).

  • Values: true (default), false (every stretch the voice detector finds counts, at the level it came in). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if soft speech close to the microphone is missed in a noisy room.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice.listen.auto_gain_snr_db

How far above the room's noise speech must be to count, in dB.

  • Values: A number of dB, 0 or more (default 6). Higher is stricter in a noisy room; 0 lets any speech through. Default: 6.
  • When to change it: Raise it if far-off talk or a TV starts stretches; lower it if you're missed when you speak softly.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.auto_gain.

voice.listen.device

Which microphone to use; empty is the system's default.

  • Values: A device's name or number as your sound system lists it (the sounddevice library's names). Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Set it if you have several microphones.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice.audio.backend

How Kurooto plays and records sound: PipeWire's own tools, or the PortAudio library inside Kurooto.

pipewire: speech plays through pw-play and the microphone is read through pw-record, separate programs, so a sound driver problem can't take Kurooto down, and the microphone follows your system's default (a source that comes and goes is picked up again). portaudio: the sounddevice library inside Kurooto, as before 0.8.16. auto picks pipewire on Linux when PipeWire is running and its tools are installed, unless voice.listen.device names a microphone (those are sounddevice's names); else portaudio. Takes effect for speaking at once, for listening the next time it starts (/voice mute, then /voice unmute).

  • Values: auto (default), pipewire or portaudio. Default: auto.
  • When to change it: Pick portaudio if speech or the microphone misbehaves through PipeWire's tools, or to use a sounddevice microphone by name; pipewire to pass a PipeWire node name in voice.listen.device.

voice.listen.levels

Show the microphone's loudness as a waveform on the dashboard (numbers only).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't watch the waveform.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice_notes.model

Which Gemini model writes out your voice notes.

  • Values: A Gemini model name. Default: gemini-flash-latest.
  • When to change it: Change it when a better or cheaper speech model is out.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Each voice note's audio goes to Google with your Gemini key.
  • Depends on: Voice notes, and a Gemini key.

voice_notes.api_base

Another address for the voice-note model; empty is Google's.

  • Values: A web address, or empty. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Only for a proxy or a compatible server of your own.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Voice notes go to that address.
  • Depends on: Voice notes.

voice.speaker_id.on

Voice ID: only your voice can make Kurooto act; others can only chat, with none of your data. Enrol first.

  • Values: true (each turn is checked against your voice print, on this computer), false (any voice is treated as yours, as before). Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if other people are ever near the computer while Voice listens. Enrol on the Your voice page first.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: About 20-60 ms of processor per thing said. Your voice print is stored encrypted on this computer, never sent anywhere.
  • Depends on: Voice: talk and listen, and an enrolled voice.

voice.speaker_id.mode

What other voices get: a chat with no actions and none of your data, or just one line saying it only talks with you.

  • Values: owner_only_acts (others may chat), owner_only_at_all (others hear one line, nothing more). Default: owner_only_acts.
  • When to change it: Pick owner_only_at_all if you don't want Kurooto chatting with anyone else at all.

voice.speaker_id.gate

Check who's speaking before writing words out: other people not calling Kurooto are never transcribed (unsure voices: once).

  • Values: true (others' talk is dropped before transcription), false (everything is transcribed, then sorted as in 0.8.8). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Leave it on: it keeps other people's conversations private and saves the transcription's processor time.

voice.speaker_id.guest_brain

Who answers other voices: your AI (none of your data, no tools) or the model on this computer.

  • Values: main (your AI, only if it can be held to plain text: an API or Claude Code; with agy or Codex the local model answers), local (always this computer). Default: main.
  • When to change it: Pick local to keep guests' words on this computer.
  • Depends on: For local, Local AI models with a chat model.

voice.speaker_id.guest_per_hour

At most this many answers to other voices an hour.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 20.
  • When to change it: Lower it if guests use up your AI's quota.

voice.speaker_id.tell_guests

Once per conversation, tell others they can chat but only you can make Kurooto act.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather it just chats without saying so.

voice.speaker_id.strictness

How sure it must be that it's you: relaxed, normal or strict (strict turns you away more often).

  • Values: relaxed, normal, strict. Default: normal.
  • When to change it: Strict if a lot of people are around; relaxed if it keeps asking you again in a noisy room.

voice.speaker_id.threshold

0: worked out from your own samples when you train. A number overrides it, held to 0.30 to 0.95.

  • Values: A number. Default: 0.0.
  • When to change it: Leave it at 0 unless the Test step on the Your voice page shows it gets it wrong.

voice.speaker_id.margin

The 'not sure' band just below the threshold, at most 0.2 wide. Not sure always counts as not you.

  • Values: A number. Default: 0.05.
  • When to change it: Widen it to be safer; narrow it if you're asked to repeat yourself too often.

voice.speaker_id.min_speech_s

Speech shorter than this is too short to tell who it is, so it counts as not sure.

  • Values: A number. Default: 1.0.
  • When to change it: Raise it if quick one-word sounds get mistaken; lower it if your short answers are often 'not sure'.

voice.speaker_id.carry

A short reply right after a turn it knew was you counts as you (not when the voice is clearly someone else).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off (or use strict) if guests could answer 'yes' for you.

voice.speaker_id.carry_s

The window, in seconds, in which a quick reply after you spoke still counts as you.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 15.
  • When to change it: Shorten it in a shared room; lengthen it if your quick follow-ups get refused.

voice.speaker_id.adapt

Improve over time: turns it was very sure were you refine your voice print, slowly and with undo.

  • Values: false (default: your voice print stays as trained), true. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if your voice changes (a cold, a new mic) and you'd rather it keeps up by itself.

voice.speaker_id.adapt_max

At most this many learned turns are kept in your voice print (the oldest are replaced).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 40.
  • When to change it: Lower it to keep your voice print closer to the samples you trained with.

voice.speaker_id.adapt_per_day

A daily cap on how many of your turns may be added to your voice print.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 10.
  • When to change it: Lower it to make the voice print change more slowly.

voice.speaker_id.model

The speaker model: campplus-en (English, the most accurate) or campplus-multi. Changing it means enrolling again.

  • Values: campplus-en, campplus-multi. Default: campplus-en.
  • When to change it: Pick campplus-multi if you speak to Kurooto mostly in another language.

voice.addons.listen

Let an add-on take one stretch of speech at a time through the microphone (written out here, never sent).

An add-on may ask for the next thing said, by any voice, while Kurooto is listening: it's written out on this computer and handed to that add-on only, never to the gate, the conversation or your AI. Mute, the night, calls and every other pause still stop it, and it never takes a conversation that's open with you.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep add-ons from hearing anything.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on, and an add-on that uses it.

voice.addons.guest_name

Let an add-on that recognises a guest name them: a greeting by name, the name to a local guest AI only.

For a turn Voice ID called a guest, a feature that knows who is in front of it (an add-on's guest_name()) may give their name. Voice puts "Hi <name>." before the first answer of the conversation, a set phrase it makes itself. The name goes into the guest's prompt only when guests are answered on this computer (voice.speaker_id.guest_brain: local, or a main AI that can't be held to text); never to your main AI, and it isn't kept, logged or put in an event.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep add-ons from naming guests.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: the add-on answers on this computer.
  • Depends on: Voice ID (voice.speaker_id.on) and an add-on that recognises people.

voice.addons.chat

Let an add-on have Kurooto chat with someone who isn't you, the guest way: no tools, none of your data.

The add-on's chat goes through the same sealed path as another voice's (Voice ID's guests): text only, no tools, none of your notes, memory or history, refusals for commands and your things, and the hourly guest limit. voice.speaker_id.guest_brain picks who answers.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep add-ons from starting chats.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: One AI turn per reply, from your main AI or this computer's model (voice.speaker_id.guest_brain).
  • Depends on: An add-on that uses it.

voice.speaker_id.spoken_enrol

During enrolment Kurooto says each step aloud. Off: the page shows the steps only.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you enrol somewhere you can't have it talk aloud.

voice.listen.conversation_mode

While another voice is here, Kurooto stays out of people talking: only its name said to it opens a talk.

  • Values: true (default with Voice ID), false (the usual gate). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Leave it on so it doesn't join your conversations with other people; nothing said then is kept.
  • Depends on: Voice ID (it knows which voice is yours).

voice.listen.conversation_s

Seconds after the other person last spoke before Kurooto stops staying out of the conversation.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 60.
  • When to change it: Lengthen it if Kurooto joins in too soon after the other person stops talking.

voice.listen.conversation_follow_s

In conversation mode, a reply to what Kurooto just said counts only this soon after it.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 4.
  • When to change it: Lengthen it if your replies to Kurooto in a conversation get ignored.

voice.listen.careful_name

When someone else may be talking, its name wakes Kurooto only said first, as a call ("Kurooto, what's ...").

Someone else may be talking when Voice ID isn't sure the voice is yours (or knows it isn't), when people talked here within voice.listen.chatter_s without talking to Kurooto, or when Face presence sees two or more faces. Then its name at the end or in the middle of a sentence, or a word that only sounds like it, is just a mention: the other rules and the model on this computer decide. With you alone, its name first or last still wakes it as before.

  • Values: true (default), false (its name first or last always wakes it, as before 0.8.13). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Leave it on if Kurooto joins your conversations when you mention it; turn it off if it ignores you at the end of a sentence when you're alone.

voice.listen.chatter_s

Speech here that wasn't to Kurooto (any voice) this many seconds ago makes it careful with its name.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 60.
  • When to change it: Lengthen it if it still jumps into a conversation that paused for a while.

voice.listen.follow_s

After Kurooto answers, what the same voice says this many seconds after counts as said to it, statements too.

You don't have to say its name again to go on: within this window of its answer ending, anything the voice it answered says goes to it. Another voice, two voices at once, or being told off ("not you", "stop") end it. In conversation mode this window applies to the voice that opened the talk instead of voice.listen.conversation_follow_s. The conversation stays open at least this long after an answer.

  • Values: Seconds; 20 (default), 0 (off: only questions and requests within voice.listen.close_s count). Default: 20.
  • When to change it: Shorten it (or 0) if it takes what you say to someone else right after its answer; lengthen it if it stops listening while you think.

voice.listen.told_off

"Stop", "not you", "never mind" and the like, right after it woke or spoke, stop it at once and close the talk.

Within about 10 seconds of the conversation opening or of Kurooto speaking: it stops speaking, closes the conversation and doesn't reply. It notes a misfire (the rule that opened it, never your words) and, with voice.listen.learn_nightly, a training row of numbers for the night's learning. With voice.listen.barge_in you can say it over Kurooto while it speaks; without it, say it as soon as it stops, or use /voice stop.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if one of those phrases is something you often say to Kurooto as a request.

voice.listen.barge_in

Talk over Kurooto while it speaks to stop it: "stop", a new question, or anything you want to say.

While Kurooto speaks it keeps listening, but never takes its own voice coming back through the room for you. A stretch of speech counts as you cutting in when Voice ID (on and enrolled) recognises your voice in it (at least about 0.6 seconds of speech, closer to your voice print than to Kurooto's own, which is made once in memory from a phrase its voice says silently), or when it's clearly louder than the echo of what's playing just then and its words, written out here, aren't Kurooto's own. Then it goes quiet at once (on this computer, or on another device an add-on plays it on), and what you said is: a told-off phrase ("stop", "never mind"): it stops; a question or a request: a new turn in the same conversation; anything else ("mm", a cough, a word to someone else): it carries on, saying the sentence it was cut in from its start (0.8.17), or stops if that isn't settled within about 12 seconds. Limits: on a speakerphone or at a high volume its echo can drown you out, so speak up or come closer; without Voice ID only the loudness check works; and with Voice ID only your voice can cut in by voice (a guest has to be louder than its echo). Nothing extra is kept: the sound stays in memory as for any stretch, and the event says only how it was noticed and what came of it.

  • Values: true (default), false (the microphone is dropped while it speaks, as before; /voice stop still stops it). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if Kurooto stops for the TV or other people talking loudly near it while it speaks.

voice.listen.aec

Echo cancelling: Kurooto's own voice taken out of what the microphone hears, so you can cut in over it.

(0.9.3) While Kurooto speaks, what it plays (its voice, the heard-you sound, Gemini Live's answers) is taken out of the microphone's sound before the listener, barge-in and Voice ID hear it, by an adaptive echo canceller (speexdsp's, with its residual echo suppressor). The delay between playing and hearing the echo is measured as it plays and followed from piece to piece (a phone that plays and hears adds a delay of its own that changes a little each time); an add-on that measured its echo hands its delay in as a start. Barge-in then expects only what's left of the echo, so your voice can cut in even where the echo is as loud as what's played (a phone's speaker beside its own microphone). Needs the speexdsp library on this computer (libspeexdsp; Linux usually has it): without it this says "unavailable" in /voice and kurooto doctor, and listening works as before. Nothing is kept: what's played and the microphone's last seconds are in memory only, and the event voice_aec has numbers only (the delay, how far down the echo was brought, the CPU it took). The first fifth of a second of a sentence played on its own (not handed over gaplessly) passes as heard while its delay is measured.

  • Values: "auto" (default: on while sound plays on an add-on's output, a phone that also hears), "on" (this computer's speakers too), "off". Default: auto.
  • When to change it: Turn it on for this computer's speakers if Kurooto cuts itself off on its own voice or never lets you in; turn it off if your voice sounds cut up to Voice ID while Kurooto speaks.

voice.listen.need_face

Answer only someone in front of it: a face close to the camera and facing it while they speak.

With Face presence on and enrolled, a turn is answered only when the camera saw a face close to it (voice.listen.face_near) and facing it (voice.listen.face_yaw_deg) while it was said or within about 1.5 seconds after. Anything else, by any rule (its name, a follow-up, the local model's judgement, cutting in), is chatter: someone on a phone call across the room, the TV, a person talking to you from the door. The wake word and the told-off phrases ("stop", "never mind") work without a face, and typed /voice commands are never affected. While Voice listens it keeps Face presence's camera looking (its card says "voice: who's talking"), never past Face presence's own pauses: a call, a locked screen, /face pause, the night, a busy or covered camera; then voice.listen.need_face_fallback decides. Only the time of each look and how many faces were close and facing are kept, in memory: no picture, no face numbers. With Face presence off or not enrolled this does nothing.

  • Values: true (default), false (the voice rules alone, as before). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you often talk to Kurooto from across the room or without looking at the screen.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: The camera's light stays on while Voice listens, and a little processor time; on Linux a video call app can't use the camera then (/voice mute or /face pause frees it).
  • Depends on: Face presence on and enrolled; Voice listening.

voice.listen.face_near

How close counts: a face at least this share of your usual face height at the desk (0.5 is about twice as far).

Your usual height comes from Face presence's recent sightings of you at the desk (or your enrolment). Until it knows, a face at least face.recognise.min_face_px tall counts.

  • Values: A number from 0.1 to 1. Default: 0.5.
  • When to change it: Lower it if it ignores you when you lean back or sit further away; raise it if it answers people across the room.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.need_face.

voice.listen.face_yaw_deg

How directly someone must face the camera: turned at most this many degrees away (an estimate).

  • Values: Degrees, 10 to 80. Default: 35.
  • When to change it: Raise it if it ignores you when you glance at a second screen; lower it if it answers people talking side-on.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.need_face.

voice.listen.need_face_fallback

What happens when the camera can't see (paused, covered, busy, no camera): the voice rules alone, or nothing.

voice: it answers by the voice rules alone, as without voice.listen.need_face. none: nothing is answered by voice until the camera can see again; the wake word and "stop" still work. /voice and /health say which applies now ("needs a face: camera paused (screen locked), using voice rules").

  • Values: voice (default) or none. Default: voice.
  • When to change it: Pick none if being answered when nobody's there is worse than not being answered.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.need_face.

voice.listen.look_to_talk

Look at the camera for a second and Kurooto starts listening, as if you'd said its name.

With Face presence on and enrolled, your face (recognised as yours), close to the camera and turned to it within voice.listen.look_yaw_deg and voice.listen.look_pitch_deg, for voice.listen.look_dwell_s, opens the conversation the way its name alone does, without a sound: the next thing you say counts as said to it, no name needed. The camera sees which way your head points, not your eyes, so the angles are tighter than voice.listen.face_yaw_deg: looking at the screen beside the camera shouldn't count. It stays open while you keep looking; look away for voice.listen.look_away_s before saying anything and it closes. A conversation already going on is left alone. One look opens it once: after it closes (you said something not to it, "stop", or looked away) look away and back. It never opens while Voice is off or muted, on a call, at night with listening off, while Kurooto is asleep, or with Face presence off or paused. What you then say still goes through the other checks: with Voice ID, someone else's voice isn't even written out; two voices at once, or people talking here lately, need a question or a request. Only two times are kept, in memory: when you started looking and when you last did.

  • Values: true (default), false (only its name, the wake word and the usual follow-ups open it). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if it starts listening while you're only reading something near the camera.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Like voice.listen.need_face, the camera stays looking while Voice listens (its light on), and a little processor time.
  • Depends on: Face presence on and enrolled; Voice listening.

voice.listen.look_dwell_s

How long you look at the camera before it starts listening: a glance shorter than this doesn't count.

Face presence looks about once a second (every 3 seconds on battery), so the wait is this long or a little more.

  • Values: Seconds, 0 to 10 (default 1). Default: 1.0.
  • When to change it: Raise it if passing glances open it; lower it to have it react sooner.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.look_to_talk.

voice.listen.look_yaw_deg

How straight at the camera your head must point, left or right: at most this many degrees (an estimate).

  • Values: Degrees, 5 to 35 (default 15). Default: 15.
  • When to change it: Raise it if looking at the camera doesn't open it; lower it if looking at the screen next to it does.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.look_to_talk.

voice.listen.look_pitch_deg

How straight at the camera your head must point, up or down, judged against your face at enrolment.

Measured from the front step of your Face presence enrolment (where you looked straight at the camera). If that isn't on record, only the left-right angle counts.

  • Values: Degrees, 5 to 35 (default 15). Default: 15.
  • When to change it: Raise it if it misses you when the camera sits above the screen; lower it if looking down at the keyboard opens it.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.look_to_talk.

voice.listen.look_away_s

Looking away this long, before you've said anything, closes it again.

  • Values: Seconds, 0 to 30 (default 3). Default: 3.0.
  • When to change it: Raise it if it closes while you glance at your notes before asking.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.look_to_talk.

voice.listen.look_gaze

With your eyes calibrated on the Face page, a look counts by your eyes on the camera, not your head's turn.

Needs the eye model (Get the eye model on the Face page: MediaPipe Iris, 2.6 MB) and Calibrate my eyes (about three seconds looking at the camera's lens). Then each frame, while Voice listens, the irises are found in your eyes (cut from the camera's full picture) and combined with your head's turn: eyes on the lens count even with your head a little turned, and eyes on the screen below the camera don't, even with your head facing it. Calibration keeps four numbers in your sealed face model (where your irises sat and how your head was turned), never a picture; Clear on the Face page drops them, and Forget drops them with the rest. Without the model or a calibration, or with this off, the head's turn counts (voice.listen.look_yaw_deg and look_pitch_deg), and /voice says which.

  • Values: true (default), false (the head's turn only). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if glasses or the light make your eyes hard to read and looks stop opening it.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: About 7 ms of one processor core per frame while Voice listens, for the eyes (measured on a Ryzen 7 laptop).
  • Depends on: voice.listen.look_to_talk; the eye model and a calibration (the Face page).

voice.listen.look_gaze_dwell_s

By the eyes: how long your eyes must be on the camera before it starts listening (0: at once).

With your eyes calibrated, eyes on the lens are a clear sign, so by default the first frame that sees them there opens the conversation (Face presence looks up to 5 times a second while Voice listens: face.watch.look_fps). Without a calibration, voice.listen.look_dwell_s applies instead.

  • Values: Seconds, 0 to 5 (default 0). Default: 0.0.
  • When to change it: Raise it a little (0.3) if a passing glance at the camera opens it.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.look_gaze and a calibration.

voice.listen.look_anyone

Anyone's look opens the conversation, not only yours: a guest looking at it can talk to it too.

Face presence reads where every close face looks (the three biggest besides yours), by the head's turn, or by the eyes judged against your calibration when you've calibrated. People talking to each other without looking at it stay ignored. A guest is still a guest: Voice ID decides, and someone who isn't you gets chat only (no tools, none of your data). Only numbers for where a face points are used, never who it is, and they go with the frame.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to have only your own look open it (as 0.8.17).
  • Depends on: voice.listen.look_to_talk, Face presence on and enrolled.

voice.listen.phone_away

Stop listening while your phone is out (it left a place you saved).

Uses Brain's phone places sensor. Listening stops when the phone leaves a saved place and starts again when it arrives at one, or when someone is back at the desk (a face Face presence knows, or the keyboard). Only that the phone left goes into it, never the place.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you'd rather the microphone rested while you're away from home.
  • Depends on: Brain on and its phone places sensor (mind.senses.phone.places).

voice.listen.look_in_company

With others here, looking at it and saying its name (even misheard) counts as calling it.

With two or more faces in view or other voices about, its name has to be said first and heard exactly. After a look, its name first or last spelled any way (a near miss too) is enough, so a garbled transcript doesn't drop what you said.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to have a look count, in company, only before a question or a request.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.look_to_talk.

voice.listen.look_gaze_yaw_deg

By the eyes: how close to the camera's lens your gaze must be, left or right (an estimate in degrees).

  • Values: Degrees, 3 to 30 (default 10). Default: 10.
  • When to change it: Raise it if looking at the camera doesn't open it; lower it if looking at a screen beside it does. The Face page shows your last look's angles while Voice listens.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.look_gaze and a calibration.

voice.listen.look_gaze_pitch_deg

By the eyes: how close to the camera's lens your gaze must be, up or down (an estimate in degrees).

  • Values: Degrees, 3 to 30 (default 10). Default: 10.
  • When to change it: Raise it if it misses you; lower it if looking at the screen just below the camera opens it.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.look_gaze and a calibration.

voice.listen.learn_nightly

Every night Kurooto learns from how its voice turns went: ones you carried on with, and ones you told off.

Each turn the gate let through is labelled once it's known how it went: you went on talking to it (good), or told it off (a misfire). Only flags and numbers are kept, in Kurooto's state under voice/: the rule that decided, how its name was said, how many voices, Voice ID's decision and score, seconds since other talk, faces in view; never words or sound. At night (04:10) a small model is fitted to them; with 20 turns or more, some of each, it can hold back a borderline opening (its name heard only as a near miss, a follow-up, the local model's judgement) that looks like past misfires. It can never stop its name said first ("Kurooto, ..."). /voice and /health show the numbers; forgetting your voice (/voice id forget) deletes them too.

  • Values: true (default), false (nothing recorded, nothing held back). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep the gate's rules fixed.

voice.speaker_id.face_assist

When Voice ID is only "not sure" it's your voice and Face presence sees just your face, it counts as you.

Only a voice already inside the "not sure" margin, only while the camera's last look (a few seconds old at most) shows exactly one face and it's yours. A voice clearly not yours, two voices, two faces, or strict strictness never count. With "improve over time" (voice.speaker_id.adapt) on, such a turn that went well may be added to your voice print like a very sure turn, with the same caps and undo.

  • Values: true (default), false. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if a photo of you plus a voice close to yours is a risk where you are.
  • Depends on: Voice ID and Face presence, both enrolled.

voice.speaker_id.carry_talk

In a talk Voice ID knew you opened, a voice it's only "not sure" of stays you, so history and tools carry.

Extends voice.speaker_id.carry: not only a short reply, but any turn whose score is inside the "not sure" margin, while the conversation you opened is open and within voice.speaker_id.carry_s or voice.listen.follow_s (the longer) of its last answer to you. A voice clearly someone else's, or two voices, are never carried; strict turns it off.

  • Values: true (default), false (only short replies carry, as before 0.8.13). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off in a shared room where someone with a voice like yours might carry on your conversation.

voice.speaker_id.per_device

Through a microphone you didn't enrol on, Voice ID learns that microphone's own print, used after your yes.

A phone's microphone (through an add-on) or a headset makes your voice score lower than at enrolment, so most turns come out "not sure".
