With this on, turns there that are proven yours (only your face in view at that moment, one voice, at least 2 seconds of speech, and a score not in the "clearly not you" band; or a "that was me" tap) keep their numbers (a vector, never sound) in your sealed voice print. With 12 of them it builds that microphone's own print, calibrated against the impostor voices heard through a simulated phone channel (never synthetic voices), and asks you once: Use it, or No (No drops them and it stops learning there). /voice id mics lists them; /voice id mic <n> use|no|forget. Until a microphone's print is in use, "improve over time" doesn't learn there (it used to drift your print toward that microphone until its guard stopped it for good).

  • Values: true (default), false. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to use only your enrolled print everywhere and keep nothing more of your voice.
  • Depends on: Voice ID enrolled; Face presence enrolled for the face-proven turns.

voice.speaker_id.per_device_per_day

At most this many face-proven turns a day are kept for a microphone's own print.

  • Values: A whole number; default 10. Default: 10.
  • When to change it: Lower it to keep fewer of your turns; the print is then built more slowly.

voice.speaker_id.claim

Tap the Body face within 10 s of a turn Voice ID wasn't sure of: it was you, and it's heard again as yours.

Only with only your face in view at the tap. The "not sure" turn's sound waits in memory for voice.speaker_id.claim_s (never written), then goes. A claim opens the talk, runs the turn as yours, and may keep its numbers: as a sample for that microphone's own print (voice.speaker_id.per_device), or with "improve over time" on, as a learned turn under its caps, drift guard and undo. At most voice.speaker_id.claim_per_day a day. A tap with nothing to claim does what it did before (voice.listen.tap_to_talk).

  • Values: true (default), false. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if someone else might tap the face while you sit in front of it.
  • Depends on: Voice ID and Face presence enrolled; an add-on that passes taps (the Body face).

voice.speaker_id.claim_s

How long after a "not sure" turn a tap can still claim it (seconds).

  • Values: Seconds; default 10. Default: 10.
  • When to change it: Shorter if you want a turn's sound gone from memory sooner; longer if the tap often comes late.

voice.speaker_id.claim_per_day

At most this many "that was me" claims a day.

  • Values: A whole number; default 5. Default: 5.
  • When to change it: Lower it if claims are being used more than you expect.

voice.speak.ack

While your AI thinks, a short soft sound says Kurooto heard you.

The moment a turn you said goes to your AI, a soft sound plays through the same speakers or output as its voice (a phone an add-on plays it on, say), so the silence while the AI works doesn't feel like it didn't hear. It's skipped when the answer is already on its way (a plain-English request done at once) or something is being said. tone is a short, quiet rising note made on this computer; word is "Hmm." in Kurooto's own voice, made once and kept in memory. Only for turns heard through the microphone; never for typed messages.

  • Values: tone (default), word or off. Default: tone.
  • When to change it: Pick word if a voice sounds friendlier to you than a note; off if you'd rather have silence.
  • Depends on: voice.speak.on and voice.listen.on.

voice.speak.stream

Speak the answer a sentence at a time while your AI is still writing it.

When your AI can stream its answer (Claude Code, a Gemini key or Ollama answering without tools), each finished sentence is spoken as soon as it arrives, instead of after the whole answer is in; the rest follows as it's written. Words the AI writes before it uses a tool ("Let me check your notes.") are spoken too. An AI that can't stream (agy) is spoken when it's done, as before. /voice stop, mute and talking over it stop it at once.

  • Values: true (default) or false (the whole answer first, as before 0.8.16). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather hear only the finished answer.
  • Depends on: voice.speak.on.

voice.speak.guests_private

With someone else at the desk, an AI answer isn't read aloud by itself: it stays on screen, and a message offers Read it aloud.

Only for answers read out because voice.speak.what is all. Someone else means two faces looking at the screen (Face presence) or a voice that isn't yours lately (Voice ID).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if answers about your work shouldn't be heard by whoever is with you.
  • Depends on: voice.speak.what = all, and Face presence or Voice ID to notice others.

voice.speak.messages

A due reminder or a question for you is also said aloud while you're alone at the desk; your spoken answer closes it.

Only with Face presence seeing you at the desk and nobody else there. The phone and Telegram copies still come. A question's answer counts only in your own voice (Voice ID), and then it's answered everywhere; "done" to a reminder takes its copies down. Approvals are never said: they wait for your tap. Each kind goes through Calm (Why?, Stop).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you'd rather hear reminders at the desk than look at the phone.
  • Depends on: Voice speaking and listening, Face presence; Voice ID for answers to close anything.

voice.listen.timing

Time each spoken turn from your last word to Kurooto's first sound, as numbers only.

Each turn that goes to your AI is timed from the end of your speech: written out, decided (the face check included), sent, the AI starting, its first words, the whole answer, the acknowledgement, the first sound made and the first sound playing (on an add-on's output: when it's handed the sound). One voice_timing event a turn (milliseconds, never words), and the medians of the last 20 turns in /voice and on GET /api/voice ("speed"). Kept in memory.

  • Values: true (default) or false. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Leave it on; turn it off only if you don't want the events in the log.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice.listen.fast_brain

Which AI answers plain chat said aloud: your AI as usual, the model on this computer, or the quickest.

Only turns that need nothing of yours and no tools are moved: small talk and general questions with none of "my", "notes", "remind", "today", "search" and the like, that no plain-English request of Kurooto's would take. Those are asked as text only at low effort. same: your AI with its tools, as always. local: the Ollama model on this computer (nothing leaves it). fastest: the quickest set up here by its first words in your recent voice turns (each is tried once first: your AI when it's a service, a Gemini key, the model on this computer, then your AI with no tools). Anything else, and every turn when unsure, goes to your AI as before.

  • Values: same (default), local or fastest. Default: same.
  • When to change it: Pick local or fastest if chatting by voice feels slow and you don't mind a smaller model for small talk.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on; local needs Local AI models (Ollama) set up.

voice.listen.warm_up

When listening starts, warm the transcriber and the voice, so your first turn isn't the slow one.

Half a second of silence is written out (on the hearing thread, never beside a turn) and one word is made by the voice and never played, once each time they're loaded. Nothing is recorded or sent.

  • Values: true (default) or false. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off on a computer short of memory if you rarely talk to it.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice.listen.warm_brain

When listening starts, get your AI's program ready on this computer.

With Claude Code as your AI, its program is run once for its version (at most every 10 minutes), so its files are read from the disk then and not on your first turn; nothing is sent. With voice.listen.fast_brain local, the model on this computer answers one word so it's loaded. After your first turn, brain.claude.session keeps Claude Code running for the conversation, so later turns don't start it again.

  • Values: true (default) or false. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't want the extra start of the AI's program.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice.listen.live

Your own turns answered by Gemini Live, speech to speech; /voice turns shows how long yours really take. Their sound goes to Google.

Instead of writing your words out, asking your AI and making the answer's voice here, the turn's sound goes to Google's Gemini Live (with your Gemini key) and its spoken answer plays through the same speakers or add-on output. A different AI answers: Google's Live model, with the tools a phone call has. What leaves the computer is the sound of the turns Kurooto already decided you said to it, and only those: the gate called it addressed (not chatter or noise), Voice ID said it's your voice, and you were facing it, close by: only turns you say to it while facing it, close by (the face check saw a face near the camera and turned to it during the turn). A turn let through without a face seen (the wake word and the request right after it, voice.listen.need_face off, Face presence off or paused, or the camera blind with voice.listen.need_face_fallback "voice") goes the usual way, as do chatter, other voices and a voice it's unsure of. With each session goes what a call's setup carries (your name, the assistant's, which tools exist, the lessons from earlier calls), and a turn's tool results (a note it reads, say). The session stays open while the conversation does and closes with it. Tools that need your tap ask Approve / Deny first. No key, Google refusing, or no sound within about 5 seconds: that turn goes the usual way. Nothing is written to disk; events hold numbers only. It uses calls.live_model (then calls.live_models) and calls.voice.

  • Values: false (default) or true. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you want quick spoken answers at the desk and are fine with your voice going to Google.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on, a Gemini key, Voice ID (voice.speaker_id.on, enrolled) and Face presence.

voice.speak.stop_when_gone

Stop reading an answer when Face presence sees you've left the desk; the rest is offered when you're back.

It goes by Face presence's "stepped away" (face.away.on, after face.away.after_min), never a turned head. The unread rest is on screen, and the greeting says an answer came while you were away; a yes reads it.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if long answers keep playing to an empty room.
  • Depends on: Face presence on with face.away.on.

voice.mirror_to_chat

What Kurooto says aloud by itself also shows in the app's and the dashboard's Chat.

A greeting or an add-on's spoken line appears as "Said aloud at your desk" (never on Telegram), and the next thing you type in any chat is told what it said, so you can answer by typing. Answers to you aren't repeated.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep spoken lines out of Chat.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: With Phone calls on, the line goes to your phone app like any chat message (sealed, through your call line).

voice.listen.count_voices

Two faces close to the camera plus a voice that isn't yours: people talking to each other, not to Kurooto.

Face presence's count of close, facing faces (in memory) and Voice ID's verdict together mark a turn as two voices: it's treated as conversation, and that person's look doesn't open a talk. Your own voice never counts as the second.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if it answers two friends talking in front of it.
  • Depends on: Face presence on and enrolled, and Voice ID.

voice.listen.tap_to_talk

A tap on an add-on's device (the Body face) opens the conversation, as its name would.

No face is needed for that turn, and Voice ID's rules for others still apply. A tap while it speaks stays a stop. The add-on has its own switch too.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if taps open conversations you didn't mean.
  • Depends on: Voice listening, and an add-on that offers it.

voice.listen.after_talk

When a talk at the desk ends, its text goes where a call's does: transcripts, open threads, a line in the day's note.

Your turns and its answers as text (never sound, never anyone else's turns). With Phone calls on they're kept with the call transcripts (forgotten like them, with their gist); "I'll send it tonight" opens a thread; the day's note gets the time and the count of your turns under "Desk talks". The AI's look back after calls doesn't run for these.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep desk talks out of the transcripts, threads and your daily note.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves this computer.

voice.listen.live_after

A turn Gemini Live answered is noticed like a typed message: threads, what you stated, Brain's tone and replies.

What App.handle notices in a typed message runs on a Live turn's words too: threads opened or closed, what you stated set up (infer.on), Brain's reply tracking and tone tag (on this computer). The turn always counts as a message from you either way.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if Live turns shouldn't open threads or set things up.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves this computer.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.live.

voice.listen.live_everyone

Every turn said to Kurooto goes to Gemini Live: yours with your tools, anyone else's to a guest session. Their voices go to Google.

With voice.listen.live on, Live normally takes only your own turns, said while you face the camera close by. This sends every turn the gate called addressed to Gemini Live, whoever said it and whether or not a face was seen. A turn Voice ID calls yours (face_assist and carry_talk count) gets your usual session with its tools. Any other turn (a voice it's unsure of, someone else's, or every turn when Voice ID is off) gets a separate guest session: no tools, nothing of yours in its setup (no name, notes, memory, history or lessons: only the assistant's name and the guest rules, with the voice.speaker_id.tell_guests line), counted against voice.speaker_id.guest_per_hour, never shared with your session; your next recognised turn closes it and goes back to yours. A request the guest path refuses (a command, something that acts or reads your things) and owner_only_at_all still go the usual way, which refuses them. Privacy: with this on, the sound of other people's turns said to Kurooto, and of your own turns Voice ID wasn't sure of, goes to Google (Gemini Live, your key), even with voice.speaker_id.guest_brain set to local. Nothing is written to disk; events hold numbers only. Failures go the usual way, as Live's do.

  • Values: false (default) or true. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on when Voice ID or the face check keeps sending your turns the slow way (a new microphone or camera) and you're fine with guests' voices going to Google.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.live and a Gemini key; Voice ID for your own session.

voice.room_modes.on

Room modes: alone, company, night or silent, set with /room, the dashboard, the phone app or by saying it.

Alone is your settings as they are. Company: nothing of yours is said aloud by itself (answers you asked for elsewhere, reminders, questions), only your own look opens a talk, and nobody else's turns go to Gemini Live. Night: the same quiet about your things, every sound quieter (voice.room_modes.night_db) and the soft tone in place of the "Hmm." word. Silent: nothing aloud at all, answers on screen only, and Gemini Live isn't used. A mode only ever turns things off or narrows them: it never turns on a feature you switched off, and never touches Voice ID, Face presence or approvals. Each ends by itself and goes back to alone.

  • Values: on or off Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep Voice exactly as its settings say, with no modes; a mode that's on ends at once.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves the computer: the mode, until when and who set it are kept in Kurooto's state.
  • Depends on: Voice. Night ends with your night hours when you've set them (availability.night).

voice.room_modes.spoken

Set a room mode by saying it ("night mode", "go silent", "I'm alone"), in your own voice only.

The whole turn has to be the phrase. It counts only when Voice ID says it's your voice; anyone else, or a voice it isn't sure of, is told to type /room. With Voice ID off it never sets a mode by voice.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you only want /room, the dashboard and the phone to change it.
  • Depends on: Room modes and Voice ID.

voice.room_modes.company_h

Company mode ends by itself after this many hours.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 3.
  • When to change it: Make it longer for a long visit; /room company 5h sets one length only.

voice.room_modes.silent_h

Silent mode ends by itself after this many hours.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 2.
  • When to change it: Make it longer if you're often in a library or a meeting for longer.

voice.room_modes.night_h

Night mode ends at the end of your night hours, or after this many hours without them.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 10.
  • When to change it: Set it to your usual night if you haven't set night hours.

voice.room_modes.night_db

At night every sound Kurooto plays is this much quieter (decibels).

  • Values: -30 (much quieter) to 0 (the same) Default: -12.
  • When to change it: Lower it if night mode is still too loud; 0 keeps night's quiet about your things but not the volume.

voice.degrade.ears

The microphone you named goes away: listening carries on from this computer's own microphone within about two seconds.

The named microphone (voice.listen.device, an add-on's phone, say) counts as gone when no sound comes, only digital silence comes, or the sound system stops listing it. Listening switches to this computer's default microphone, Voice ID uses the voice print made on that one, /voice and /health say "ears source lost", and it goes back when the named one is listed again.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you'd rather it keep hearing you when the phone is unplugged than stop listening.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: This computer's own microphone listens while the named one is gone (sound stays on this computer, as always).
  • Depends on: Voice listening with a microphone named in voice.listen.device.

voice.degrade.piper

Kokoro fails to load or to speak: Piper speaks instead, only when it's already set up.

What Kokoro didn't say yet is said by Piper; Kokoro is tried again ten minutes later. Piper is never fetched for this: without it the answers stay on screen only. /voice and /health say which it's using.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep only the voice you chose (answers on screen when it fails).
  • Depends on: Voice speaking with Kokoro; Piper set up earlier (voice.speak.engine piper once).

voice.listen.trace

One numbers-only row per stretch heard, the last 20 in memory: /voice turns, the dashboard, add-ons here.

For each stretch of speech: what the gate called it (addressed, chatter, noise, told off) and by which rule, the face check's state (seen, none, blind, off, free), Voice ID's word and score, which way the turn went (Gemini Live, the guest session, your AI, the guest path, dropped, lost), and how long each stage took in milliseconds from your last word: Voice ID's check, the transcriber's wait and work and how many times it ran, the gate, sent, the first sound. Plus the machine's load. Never words or sound; kept in memory only (never in the event log or on disk). /voice turns shows the table with p50 and p90; the dashboard's hearing panel shows the same in short; add-ons on this computer read it (Voice.turn_log).

  • Values: true (default) or false. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't want even these numbers kept while Kurooto runs.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves this computer.

voice.listen.stage_deadline_s

A turn whose words took longer than this to come through gets "Sorry, I lost you there. Say that again?" instead of a late answer.

When the computer is busy, writing your words out and judging them can take many seconds, and an answer half a minute later is worse than none. Past this many seconds from your last word to the moment the turn would be sent, nothing is sent: it says "Sorry, I lost you there. Say that again?" and listens on, so you can say it again without its name. It never hands the turn to another AI. /voice turns shows such a turn as "lost".

  • Values: Seconds; 0 (default) is off. About 8 is a good value. Default: 0.
  • When to change it: Set it when you've had answers arrive long after you asked.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves this computer.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.on.

voice.listen.wait_unfinished

When your words stop on an unfinished note ("and", "so", a trailing comma) it waits up to 1.4 s for the rest.

Normally 0.7 seconds of quiet ends what you're saying. With this on, the words written out at your first pause are looked at: if they stop on a joining word ("and", "so", "but", "because", "the" ...) or a comma, it waits up to 1.4 seconds of quiet before taking the turn, so a pause to think doesn't cut you off. While those words are still being written out it waits too (at most 1.4 s).

  • Values: false (default) or true. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if it often answers half a sentence.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves this computer.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.early.

voice.listen.live_prewarm

Open Gemini Live's connection ahead when Face presence recognises you looking at it, or your wake word in your voice.

A turn that goes to Gemini Live first waits for the connection and its setup (often most of a second). With this on, the connection to your own Live session is opened ahead, with no sound, when Face presence recognises your face looking at the camera, or hears your wake word in your voice (Voice ID); never for anyone else's face and never the guest session. Nothing you say is sent until the gate has judged a turn said to Kurooto. An unused connection closes after 30 seconds; it opens at most 6 times in 10 minutes; one idle for a few seconds is checked (a ping) before your turn uses it. Privacy: your session's setup (the instructions a call's setup carries: your name, the tools, the lessons from earlier calls) goes to Google when it opens, even if you then say nothing.

  • Values: false (default) or true. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on when Live answers but the first word takes long to come.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Your session's setup goes to Google (your Gemini key) a little more often; no sound.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.live, Face presence (enrolled) or a wake word, and Voice ID.

voice.listen.fast_live

The fast way to Live: your face looking at the camera and talking opens Live while you speak, and the gate judges at your first pause.

When Face presence sees your face looking at the camera and your mouth moving during what you say (from the five points its face model gives: how far your mouth's corners sit below your eyes and how far apart they are, as a change from frame to frame), Live's connection is opened right then (no sound), and at your first short pause the gate judges the words so far, the local model too where the rules need it, and Voice ID's check is made then. When you then stop with nothing more said, the turn goes to Live at once: no second check, no second judgement, no connection to wait for. If you go on after the pause, it's judged as usual on all of it. Only turns the gate judged said to Kurooto are ever sent; chatter, noise and other people's talk never are. Measured with stand-ins: from your last word to Live's first sound about 0.8 s sooner (a 0.6 s connection and a 0.4 s local model). The mouth reading is rough: five points at a few frames a second.

  • Values: false (default) or true. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on with Live when you want its answers to start sooner.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Like voice.listen.live_prewarm: your session's setup goes to Google when you look at it and talk, before the turn is judged; no sound until it is.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.live, voice.listen.early, Face presence (enrolled), and Voice ID for the check at the pause.

voice.live.breaker.on

After two failed Live connections in 10 minutes, Live rests: turns go the usual way at once instead of waiting 5 s.

Only connections Google refused (or that couldn't be made) count, never a slow first sound. While resting, /voice and the dashboard say "Live resting", each turn goes the usual way without trying Live, and once a minute a connection with an empty setup (a model name, nothing of yours) is tried; when one works, Live is back.

  • Values: false (default) or true. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if your network or Google's Live service is often down and turns wait for it.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: A connection with an empty setup once a minute while resting.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.live.

voice.live.ack

A soft sound on a Live turn when Live hasn't answered 0.6 s after you stopped, and when it runs a tool.

The same sound as voice.speak.ack (the tone, or the word), through the same speakers or add-on output, and cut off the moment Live's own sound starts. The page shows it heard you either way; in a quiet room mode (night, silent) only that.

  • Values: false (default) or true. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if Live's pause before answering leaves you wondering whether it heard.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.live.

voice.live.resume

Live keeps your conversation's context for 10 minutes after it closes (your own session only).

Your Live session asks Google for a resumption handle, kept in memory only; when your session opens again within 10 minutes (a new conversation, or Google ending the connection), it continues the old one, so "and the one after that?" still makes sense. Never for the guest session; a handle Google no longer takes is dropped.

  • Values: false (default) or true. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if Live forgets what you just talked about when you come back to it.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Google keeps your session's context for the resumption window.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.live.

voice.live.spoken_max_s

Live stops speaking at the first sentence end past this many seconds; the rest is on screen.

Past that many seconds of Live's sound, it stops at the next sentence end (worked out from Live's own words as it says them), and the rest of the answer is shown, not said.

  • Values: Seconds; 0 (default) is off. About 12 is a good value. Default: 0.
  • When to change it: Set it if Live gives long spoken answers at the desk.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.live.

Privacy and what it keeps

Where Kurooto may reach on the internet, how long it keeps transcripts, requests and chats, and the one-line gists conversations settle into as their words fade. Every request Kurooto makes is counted by host; /egress shows them. These settings only ever keep less or reach less. More in Privacy: what stays and what leaves.

For every setting in this group, unless its entry says otherwise:

  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: these settings decide what is kept and where requests may go; they send nothing themselves.
  • Depends on: Nothing: core settings, always there.

privacy.egress

Count every request Kurooto makes on the internet, also refuse hosts not on the list, or neither.

  • Values: record (count everything; a host not on the list is counted and still reached), enforce (refuse such hosts, naming them and how to allow them), off. Default: record.
  • When to change it: Look at /egress for a while on record, allow what you trust, then switch to enforce. Add-ons by link are fenced to their own hosts in every mode.

privacy.allow_hosts

More hosts Kurooto may reach, besides its list and the addresses you set up yourself.

  • Values: A list of host names: ["feeds.example.org"]; .example.org allows all of it. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Add a host /egress shows as not on the list and that you trust, before turning on enforce.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Kurooto may reach those hosts.

privacy.keep_days

Transcripts, AI requests and answers, chats and logs are deleted after this many days; 0 keeps them.

  • Values: Days; 0: kept until you /forget. Default: 0.
  • When to change it: Set it (say, 30) if you don't want old conversations kept. Other features' own retention follows it where they say so.

privacy.keep_gist

As a conversation's words fade, it settles into one line of what it decided (/gists).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep nothing once the words are gone.

privacy.gist_days

Gists are deleted after this many days; 0 keeps them until you /forget all.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 0.
  • When to change it: Set it if even one-line gists shouldn't be kept for long.
  • Depends on: privacy.keep_gist.

privacy.gist_model

A model on this computer adds a sentence to a call's gist.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on for richer call gists; it never uses a cloud AI.
  • Depends on: privacy.keep_gist and Local AI models.

Updates, backups and upkeep

How Kurooto updates itself, the daily backup zip, Profile sync (your setup, encrypted, in your own private repository), the Space keeper that clears Kurooto's own leftovers, and the daily health pass. Kurooto only ever removes its own files: never your notes, books or anything else in a folder you chose. More in Updates, backups and moving.

For every setting in this group, unless its entry says otherwise:

  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Updates are downloaded from Kurooto's release site and checked against its signing key. Backups stay on the drive or folder you pick. Profile sync goes, encrypted, to your own private GitHub repository.
  • Depends on: Updates and health are core; Backups, Profile sync and the Space keeper are features of their own.

updates.mode

How updates happen: by themselves at night, after asking you, or not at all.

  • Values: night (download when found, switch in the night window), ask (a message with Update / Later), off (only a note on the dashboard). Default: night.
  • When to change it: Pick ask if you want to choose when; off only if you update by hand (kurooto update).
  • Depends on: Nothing: core.

updates.window

The hours an automatic update may switch versions.

  • Values: A pair of times: ["03:00", "05:00"]. Default: ["03:00", "05:00"].
  • When to change it: Move it to hours the computer is on and you're not using it.
  • Depends on: updates.mode = night.

updates.channel

Stable releases, or beta for every version the day it's signed.

  • Values: Text. Default: stable.
  • When to change it: Pick beta to try new things first (and report problems); stable for an older, steadier version.
  • Depends on: Nothing: core.

updates.staged

Wait until a release has been out a while (and not during exams) before installing it; critical ones don't wait.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you'd rather let others find the bugs first.
  • Depends on: Nothing: core.

updates.wait_hours

Hours a new version must have been out before it's installed.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 48.
  • When to change it: Raise it to wait longer.
  • Depends on: updates.staged.

updates.exam_hold

Nothing installs in an exam period or within a day of a quiz or exam.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if updates during exams are fine.
  • Depends on: updates.staged; exam dates from Subjects and Deadlines.

backups.folder

Where the backup zips go: an external drive or a cloud-synced folder.

  • Values: A full folder path. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Set it to a drive that isn't this computer's own disk, so a dead disk doesn't take the backup with it.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: If the folder is synced by a cloud service, the zips go there.
  • Depends on: Backups.

backups.when

When the automatic backup runs.

  • Values: A schedule: daily HH:MM (several: daily 07:00,13:00), weekly sun 19:00, hourly or every 15m. Default: daily 03:30.
  • When to change it: Move it to when the computer is on and the drive is plugged in.
  • Depends on: Backups.

backups.keep

How many backup zips to keep; Kurooto removes only its own older zips.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 7.
  • When to change it: Raise it to go back further; lower it to save space.
  • Depends on: Backups.

backups.notes

Include your notes folder in the backup.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on unless Notes sync or something else already backs up your notes.
  • Depends on: Backups.

backups.secrets

Include passwords and keys (bot token, API keys, sign-ins): anyone with the zip can use them.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on only if the backup folder is somewhere only you can reach. It's what lets a restore come back fully signed in.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Your secrets sit unencrypted in the zip.
  • Depends on: Backups.

backups.index

Include the notes brain's index (big, but slow to rebuild).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep zips small; the index is rebuilt after a restore.
  • Depends on: Backups.

backups.memory

Include the memory ledger (encrypted: it opens only where its key is).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on to keep gists and threads across a restore; on another computer it also needs backups.secrets.
  • Depends on: Backups.

backups.confidential_mail

Include confidential mail summarised on this computer (the Mail digest's local mode).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Leave it off unless you need those summaries back after a restore.
  • Depends on: Backups, and mail_digest.confidential = local.

profile.repo

Your private GitHub repository for Profile sync; empty uses the one Notes sync uses.

  • Values: owner/name, or empty. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Set it to keep your profile apart from your notes' repository.
  • Depends on: Profile sync.

profile.when

When your profile is saved each day.

  • Values: A schedule: daily HH:MM (several: daily 07:00,13:00), weekly sun 19:00, hourly or every 15m. Default: daily 04:40.
  • When to change it: Move it to when the computer is usually on.
  • Depends on: Profile sync.

profile.after_changes

Minutes after you change a setting (or turn a feature on or off) that the profile is saved.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 10.
  • When to change it: Lower it to save sooner after changes.
  • Depends on: Profile sync.

profile.branch

The branch your saved profile lives on (set by Kurooto).

  • Values: Text. Default: kurooto-profile.
  • When to change it: Never by hand.
  • Depends on: Profile sync.

profile.remote

For tests only: a local repository instead of GitHub.

  • Values: Text. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Never in normal use.
  • Depends on: Profile sync.

space.when

When the Space keeper clears Kurooto's own leftovers by itself.

  • Values: A schedule: daily HH:MM (several: daily 07:00,13:00), weekly sun 19:00, hourly or every 15m. Default: weekly sun 04:30.
  • When to change it: Move it to when the computer is on. /space cleans now.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves.
  • Depends on: The Space keeper.

space.cache_days

Downloads, made zips and cut-off downloads older than this (days) go.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 14.
  • When to change it: Lower it on a small disk.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves.
  • Depends on: The Space keeper.

space.log_mb

A log file bigger than this (MB) is cut to its newest part.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 20.
  • When to change it: Raise it to keep more history for troubleshooting.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves.
  • Depends on: The Space keeper.

space.jobs_days

Finished AI requests older than this (days) are forgotten.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 60.
  • When to change it: Raise it if you search old requests with /history.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves.
  • Depends on: The Space keeper.

space.undo_days

The concept graph's before-copies and unfinished work files older than this (days) go.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 30.
  • When to change it: Raise it to keep Undo for the concept graph longer.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves.
  • Depends on: The Space keeper.

space.backups_max_gb

The most the backup zips may take in all (GB); 0: no limit. The newest is never removed.

  • Values: A number. Default: 3.0.
  • When to change it: Set it to fit your backup drive.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves.
  • Depends on: The Space keeper and Backups.

space.backups_max_age_days

Backup zips older than this (days) go; 0: no limit (backups.keep still applies).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 0.
  • When to change it: Set it if you only care about recent backups.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves.
  • Depends on: The Space keeper and Backups.

health.on

The daily health pass: it checks what matters and tells you only when something needs you.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to stop the daily pass; /health, the dashboard and kurooto doctor still check.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves beyond the message.
  • Depends on: Nothing: core.

health.when

When the daily health pass runs; tonight's alarm is checked then.

  • Values: A schedule: daily HH:MM (several: daily 07:00,13:00), weekly sun 19:00, hourly or every 15m. Default: daily 21:00.
  • When to change it: Keep it before bedtime so a broken alarm is found in time.
  • Depends on: health.on.

health.auto_fix

Try a safe fix before telling you (never a sync: that one waits for your tap).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to have every fix wait for your tap.
  • Depends on: health.on.

This computer

How Kurooto behaves on this computer: starting with it, battery and sleep, which local models and programs it installs for you, its dashboard's port, the features list, and running every nightly process at once. More in Your computer.

For every setting in this group, unless its entry says otherwise:

  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves this computer, except downloads of the models and programs you ask it to install.
  • Depends on: Nothing: core settings, always there.

modules.enabled

The features that are on. The Features page changes it.

  • Values: A list of feature ids. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Use the Features page (or kurooto enable/disable), which sets each feature up properly; editing this list by hand skips that.

power.on_battery

What Kurooto does on battery: put off heavy work, carry on as usual, or pause.

  • Values: saver (heavy work waits for the charger; Telegram and reminders keep working), normal (as if plugged in), pause (nothing runs until you plug in; the call line still rings the alarm). Default: saver.
  • When to change it: Pick normal on a laptop with a big battery; pause if you want it quiet whenever you're unplugged.

autostart.when

When Kurooto starts: when you log in, or (Linux with systemd) when the computer starts, before anyone logs in.

  • Values: One of: login; boot (before anyone logs in (Linux with systemd)). Default: login.
  • When to change it: Pick boot for a home server or a computer you rarely log in to. Settings → Starting does it and says what it did.

awake.lid

Linux: keep running with the lid closed while plugged in.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you keep the laptop shut on a desk. Elsewhere it's the system's own lid setting.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Power while the lid is closed.
  • Depends on: Stay awake on charger.

hardware.force_profile

Use another hardware profile's models than the one detected for this computer.

  • Values: Empty (detected), light, apple-8, apple, small-gpu, big-gpu, big-gpu-12. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Pick a lighter profile if local models make this computer slow; a bigger one only if the detection undersold it.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: A different profile may download different Ollama models.
  • Depends on: Local AI models.

hardware.extra_models

Ollama models of your own to install, past what suits this computer.

  • Values: A list of Ollama model names: ["gemma4:12b"]. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Add a model you want to use (in brain.models or elsewhere).
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Each one is downloaded from Ollama's registry, and takes disk space.
  • Depends on: Local AI models.

hardware.extra_prereqs

Other programs Kurooto should install for you, by id.

  • Values: A list of ids: tesseract, poppler, tectonic, git… Default: none.
  • When to change it: Add a program a feature you use would benefit from (Tesseract for scanned books, say).
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Each one is downloaded from its project's site or your system's package source.

web.port

The dashboard's port on this computer (read when Kurooto starts).

  • Values: A port number. Default: 8770.
  • When to change it: Change it only if another program uses 8770. Restart Kurooto afterwards; bookmarks need the new port.

runall.on

/run all and kurooto run all: every nightly process that's on, now, one after another.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you never want the nightly work run on demand.

uninstall.offer

When you turn a feature off, ask whether to remove what Kurooto installed for it (a model, a program), with its size.

  • Values: true (ask, Remove or Keep; nothing goes without your tap), false (never ask; kurooto installed and kurooto remove still work). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you switch features on and off a lot and would rather clear things up yourself.
  • Depends on: Only what's on Kurooto's install receipt is ever offered: never something that was there before Kurooto.

uninstall.with_programs

kurooto uninstall also removes the programs and models Kurooto installed for you, not only Kurooto itself.

  • Values: true (everything on the install receipt goes too), false (only Kurooto, its settings and data; Ollama, Node and the rest stay). Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off before uninstalling if you want to keep using Ollama, Node or the other programs on your own.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: With it on, models you pulled through Kurooto are deleted and have to be downloaded again if you want them back.

reserve.today

Keep today.md in the reserve folder: today's alarm, classes, what's due today and tomorrow and events, for every AI request to read.

A plain file in Kurooto's state folder (reserve/), rewritten when its contents change. Lines about health or money are left out, and nothing about your mood or the senses is in it.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather your AI read only the separate files.

Dashboard and terminal

How the dashboard looks and what's on its board: the design, the board, colours, panes, the being, Study mode and the talk box; and the Kurooto Terminal, a real terminal window laid out like the dashboard's terminals. Look changes show at once (the palette reloads the page). More in The dashboard.

For every setting in this group, unless its entry says otherwise:

  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves this computer. Fonts and colours come with Kurooto; nothing is fetched.
  • Depends on: Nothing: the dashboard's own settings, always there.

look.design

The dashboard's design: new (sans-serif, rounded panes) or classic (the monospace look of 0.5.18).

  • Values: One of: new; classic (the monospace look of 0.5.18). Default: new.
  • When to change it: Pick classic if you prefer a terminal look; the board, keys and data are the same in both.

look.board

The board: Kurooto as one living body (being), three panes on top (three), or STATUS on the left (classic).

  • Values: One of: being (Kurooto as one living body, its work lighting up as it happens (0.8.9)); three (NOTES, SYSTEMS and BRAIN on top, STATUS and TERMINAL below); classic (STATUS down the left (before 0.8.3)). Default: being.
  • When to change it: Pick three or classic on an older computer or if you'd rather see plain panes.

look.layout

Dense (everything on one full-screen board) or roomy (bigger panes and type).

  • Values: dense (screens 1280 px wide or more; narrower ones get roomy) or roomy. Default: dense.
  • When to change it: Pick roomy on a small screen or if you want bigger type.

look.palette

The colours of Kurooto's pages and terminals: its own palettes, your desktop's, or a named theme.

  • Values: kuro, rose, system (your desktop's accent and light or dark mode), or a named theme such as a Catppuccin flavour. Default: kuro.
  • When to change it: Pick the one you like; the dots under it preview the dashboard's and the light pages' colours. The page reloads to take it.

look.theme_file

A colours file of your own (a kitty theme, or matugen or pywal JSON) instead of the desktop's.

  • Values: A full path, or empty. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Set it if your desktop's colours come from a file; it switches the palette to system. Empty it to stop.

look.panes

Panes hidden from the dense board: status, systems, notes, terminal.

  • Values: A list of pane names; empty: none hidden. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Hide panes you never look at (the panes menu on the board does the same).

look.brain_monitor

BRAIN's part monitor: shown beside the model, or in a drawer behind a tab.

  • Values: One of: shown (the part monitor stays beside the model); drawer (it slides out from a tab). Default: shown.
  • When to change it: Pick drawer for more room for the model.

look.being_theme

The being's colours: white light on black, or the page's palette.

  • Values: One of: void (white light on black); theme (the page's palette). Default: void.
  • When to change it: Pick theme to have the being match your palette.
  • Depends on: look.board = being.

look.being_hints

The being's hints: what to click and what each part is (H on the board toggles them).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn them off once you know your way around.
  • Depends on: look.board = being.

look.being_resting_sparks

The being's resting sparks: a low trickle in the brain from what Kurooto does all the time.

Each reading of its body (every 2 seconds) is a spark in the insula, one more for each quarter of the processor in use; its own processor use keeps the thalamus going in proportion; a timer of the scheduler that fires lights its feature's region; changed notes light the hippocampus, sound heard the auditory cortex. Events (AI requests, messages, syncs) spark either way.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather the brain sparked only for events. Remove animations and the system's reduced motion turn it off too.
  • Depends on: look.board = being.

look.being_live_waveform

The being's YOU lane live: a trace of the microphone's loudness as you speak, the last six seconds.

While Voice listens and the dashboard is open, the microphone's loudness (five numbers every 100 ms, never sound or words) scrolls through the YOU lane, newest at the right; when your turn ends it settles into that turn's outline, and with nothing heard for a moment and a half the trace fades out. Remove animations and the system's reduced motion keep it still: it sweeps across instead of scrolling. Off, the lane shows only each turn's outline once it's over.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if the moving trace distracts you.
  • Depends on: look.board = being, Voice listening, and voice.listen.levels on.

look.being_status

The being as a glance: eyes shut in focus or asleep, the held count by the heart, a glow on the deadline it's uneasy about.

From Brain's status stream (mind.status.on). Tap the glow to open that deadline (Today) or promise (the Brain console); on the phone's organ pages the same shows as a line under the organ's name. The phone's whole being and organ pages show it too.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather the being didn't show Brain's state.
  • Depends on: look.board = being, Brain on.

look.being_mood

Brain's own mood in the drawing: the fluid's tint and the heart's beat; its pupil and pulse show the three knobs.

A low mood is only quieter (dimmer, a softer, slower beat), never a sad face. Arousal quickens the pulse; the pupil widens with how much it explores and narrows a little while its beliefs are no better than a guess. It's Brain's mood, not a reading of you.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep the being's colours and beat as they were.
  • Depends on: look.board = being, Brain on.

look.talk_box

A small box over the board to talk to Kurooto in plain English (Alt+K).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you use the terminal or Telegram instead.

look.chat_page

The Chat page (Alt+H): the phone app's chat on the dashboard, live, with its buttons, and a box to write in it.

The same chats and messages as the app: Kurooto's messages and answers with their buttons, files as links, the app's Waiting for you questions, and what you wrote in the app or on this page. A tap or a message here goes the same way as one in the app, so a question settles everywhere and the chat's AI context carries. Off, the page leaves the dashboard's pages and its live messages stop.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you only use the chat on your phone or Telegram.
  • Depends on: Phone calls on (the chat comes with the phone app).

dashboard.chat_attach

On the Chat page: paste a picture (Ctrl+V), drop files or pick them with the paperclip to send with a message.

They wait above the box with a small preview and × to take one off, then go with your next message: up to 10 at a time, 20 MB each. Your AI gets them as a photo or a file sent on Telegram: told where each is, a picture as a picture. A picture is told by its bytes, not its name. They're kept in Kurooto's state folder (chat-files/), never in your notes, until you delete them; your phones see each file's name and size only. Off, the paperclip goes, nothing more can be attached, and the AI no longer reads that folder.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you never send files from the dashboard, or don't want them kept on this computer.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: A file you attach goes to the AI you picked with that message, as a file sent on Telegram does.
  • Depends on: look.chat_page on, and Phone calls (the chat comes with the phone app).

look.settings_guide

On the Settings page, a line under each setting that says what it does and opens its whole guide entry.

With it on, the search box also looks through the entries, so "battery" finds power.on_battery. Off, the page shows only the settings, with what each does when you hover its name.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off once you know your settings and want a shorter page; the guide stays in the user wiki.

look.today_class_actions

Today's classes on the dashboard get skip / going buttons (the same as /skip and /attend).

Skip marks today's class as skipped (calls may come during it, the evening check-in doesn't ask about it); going undoes that. Only what you tap: nothing is guessed.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep the Classes list read-only.
  • Depends on: Subjects.

look.study_extras

The Study pane's Open box: the subject's open doubts, and the concept Brain thinks you're ready to learn next.

Doubts come from the Doubt queue (with it on). The next concept comes from Brain's model of what you know (mind.tom.on): a concept not mastered whose prerequisites are, the subject's own first. Worked out on this computer.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off for a shorter Study pane.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves this computer.

look.jobs_source

The AI jobs page says where each request came from (Telegram, the phone chat, the talk box, voice, by itself).

A request from one of the phone app's chats links to that chat on the Chat page. Only the kind of place is kept (a word per request), never what was asked.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off for the shorter table.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves this computer.

look.notes_mastery

The NOTES map rings concept notes by what Brain thinks you know: known well, partly, or ready to learn next.

A ring over the neighbourhood's colour (green: known well; sand: partly; double peach: ready next), and the grade in words when you point at a note. From Brain's model of what you know (mind.tom.on), worked out on this computer.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off for the plain map.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing leaves this computer.
  • Depends on: Brain with mind.tom.on.

look.study_mode

The dashboard in Study mode: the notes map, the study timer and what's due (Alt+M, /study mode).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Usually switched with Alt+M rather than here.
  • Depends on: The Study tracker for the timer's log.

look.projects

Folders of git repositories the PROJECTS box shows; empty uses Research's repositories.

  • Values: A list of full paths. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Set it if your projects aren't in Research's list.

look.hidden_features

Features hidden from the Features list (they still run if they're on).

  • Values: A list of feature ids. Default: none.
  • When to change it: Usually set by hiding a card on the Features page; a search there still finds hidden ones.

look.links_in

Where links to other sites open: this computer's default browser, or the browser showing the dashboard.

  • Values: default or here. Default: default.
  • When to change it: Pick here if the dashboard runs in a browser that isn't your default.

terminal.tabs

The Kurooto Terminal's tabs, in order: your AI's program, your shell, Kurooto chat, the log.

  • Values: A list of agent, shell, chat, logs, and top. Default: ["agent", "shell", "chat", "logs"].
  • When to change it: Drop tabs you don't use, or reorder them.
  • Depends on: Kurooto Terminal.

terminal.top

Where kurooto top goes: a column beside the first tab, a tab of its own, or nowhere.

  • Values: One of: left (a column beside the first tab); tab (a tab of its own); off. Default: left.
  • When to change it: Pick tab on a narrow screen.
  • Depends on: Kurooto Terminal.

terminal.shell

Which shell the terminal runs: fish if you have it, else yours, or one you name.

  • Values: auto, or a program such as zsh or pwsh. Default: auto.
  • When to change it: Name your shell if auto picks the wrong one.
  • Depends on: Kurooto Terminal.

terminal.font_size

The Kurooto Terminal's font size.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 11.
  • When to change it: Raise it on a high-resolution screen.
  • Depends on: Kurooto Terminal.

terminal.kitty

Linux and macOS: use kitty (yours, or Kurooto's copy); off uses your usual terminal, a window per tab.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather not install kitty.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Kurooto's copy of kitty is downloaded once, if you don't have one.
  • Depends on: Kurooto Terminal.

terminal.default

Make Kurooto Terminal your default terminal (turning it off undoes it).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you'd like every new terminal in Kurooto's layout and colours.
  • Depends on: Kurooto Terminal.

terminal.default_opens

As your default terminal, what a launch opens: one shell tab, or the whole window.

  • Values: One of: shell (one tab); layout (all tabs). Default: shell.
  • When to change it: Pick layout to get every tab each time.
  • Depends on: terminal.default.

look.memory_page

The dashboard's Memory page (Alt+U): everything Kurooto keeps about you, side by side.

The memory ledger, open threads, what was set up from what you said, what it learned from its own work, topics you aren't interested in, Brain's model of you (with Wrong and Forget) and what add-ons keep, as counts. It reads; each list's own command changes it, with Undo. Nothing on it is sent anywhere.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to hide the page.

look.being_held_link

The being's held count opens the Waiting page (Alt+W): every held message in full, with Send now.

Also the "while you were away" card and how many questions and proposals wait. It reads on this computer; Send now sends the held messages as the evening bundle would.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to hide the page and make the count a plain number again.

Add-ons

Your own add-ons folder (extra features, skills, prompts and default settings laid over Kurooto), and add-ons by link from someone else's repository at a pinned commit, each fenced to the hosts on its card. Add-ons run as you, with your permissions: turn on only ones you wrote or read. More in Add-ons, scripts and other programs.

For every setting in this group, unless its entry says otherwise:

  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Your own add-ons run as you and may reach whatever they're written to. Add-ons by link reach only the hosts on the card you agreed to.
  • Depends on: Your add-ons (overlay) and Add-ons by link are features of their own.

overlay.path

The full path of your add-ons folder.

  • Values: A full folder path. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Point it at the folder with your modules, skills, prompts and settings.json. Read when Kurooto starts.
  • Depends on: Your add-ons.

overlay.code

Also run the Python add-ons in its modules/ folder (off: only skills, prompts and settings).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to use an add-ons folder's skills and settings without running its code.
  • Depends on: Your add-ons.

addons.on

Load the add-ons you added by link (kurooto addons add); off, none load from the next start.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to stop all of them at once; they stay where they are.
  • Depends on: Add-ons by link.

addons.official_updates

Say once when a newer version of an official add-on you have is out; it's never updated by itself.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to stop the message (the update buttons stay on the card).
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing beyond the message: Kurooto's own list says what's newer.
  • Depends on: Add-ons by link.

The phone app's own settings

Settings that live on the phone itself, in the Android app's Settings, not on your computer: how the app looks, its ringtones, bedside mode, location reminders, and the senses it may share. Long-press any of them in the app for its entry here. More in Your phone.

For every setting in this group, unless its entry says otherwise:

  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Nothing: these stay on the phone, except the senses, which go sealed to your computer when you switch them on.
  • Depends on: The Android app, paired with your computer.

app.vivid

Vivid colours from your wallpaper; off uses the wallpaper's quieter colours (Calm).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off for a quieter app.

app.floatingBar

The navigation bar floats above the screen; off, it sits on the bottom edge.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if the floating bar covers things you want to tap.

app.moving

The moving background; off, a still picture.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to save battery or if motion bothers you (Android's Remove animations stops it too).

app.newCallScreen

The new call screen (ringing and, since 0.8.16, the call itself); off, the call screens from before 0.8.7.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off only if the new screen misbehaves on your phone.

app.direct

Connect straight to your call line; off, calls and messages come through ntfy.sh only.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off only if the straight connection keeps dropping on your network.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Off, wake-up pushes (no content) go through ntfy.sh.

app.viewMode

Which view the Views tab opens on.

  • Values: systems or notes (the Notes map). Default: systems.
  • When to change it: Pick the one you look at most.
  • Depends on: calls.views on your computer.

app.viewsOrgans

Views shows the being as four pages, Brain, Heart, Senses and Memory, each organ alone with its parts listed under it.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to see the whole being in one drawing, with the dashboard's panels, as before 0.8.10's organ pages.
  • Depends on: calls.views and calls.views_being on your computer.

app.viewOrgan

Which organ page Views opens on: the one you looked at last.

  • Values: brain, heart, senses or memory. Default: brain.
  • When to change it: Nothing to set: tap another page and Views remembers it.
  • Depends on: app.viewsOrgans.

app.callTone

The ringtone for Kurooto's calls; empty is the phone's default.

  • Values: A ringtone picked from the phone's list. Default: the phone's default ringtone.
  • When to change it: Pick one you'll recognise as Kurooto.

app.alarmTone

The tone for the wake-up alarm; it plays on the alarm stream, so it rings even on silent.

  • Values: A tone picked from the phone's list. Default: the phone's default alarm.
  • When to change it: Pick one loud enough to wake you.

app.bedsideAuto

Open bedside mode by itself while charging between the hours you set.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on for a phone that lives on a charger by your bed.

app.bedsideFrom

When bedside mode may start opening by itself.

  • Values: A time HH:MM. Default: 22:00.
  • When to change it: Set it to when you go to bed.
  • Depends on: app.bedsideAuto.

app.bedsideTo

When bedside mode stops opening by itself.

  • Values: A time HH:MM. Default: 07:00.
  • When to change it: Set it to when you get up.
  • Depends on: app.bedsideAuto.

app.bedsideKeepOn

Keep the screen on while bedside mode is open and charging.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to let the screen sleep; bedside mode still dims at night.

app.bedsideToday

Bedside mode also shows today's reminders, what's due within a day and that Kurooto is asleep.

From the Today list your computer sealed for this phone. Locked with a PIN or pattern: only how many.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off for the plain clock, alarm and calls.

phone.bedside.signal

Your phone tells this computer when bedside mode opens or closes (a flag, no sensor).

Kept as on or off with its time (calls/bedside.json) and an event; one sent while the computer was off is dropped. Nothing acts on it unless a feature that uses it is turned on.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if this computer shouldn't know.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: The flag goes sealed through your call line, which passes it on.
  • Depends on: A paired Android phone (0.9.0 or later).

app.places.on

Location reminders: "remind me when I reach the library" for the places you saved.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on (Places) and save your places on the phone.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Where your places are never leaves the phone; your computer only sees their names.
  • Depends on: reminders.places on your computer, and location permission.

app.senses.screen

Share when the screen is on and how often you unlock (counts only).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Switch it on if you want Brain's timing to know when you're on your phone.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed with this phone's key, through your call line, every 15 minutes.
  • Depends on: Brain on your computer, with mind.senses.phone for this sensor.

app.senses.charging

Share whether the phone is charging.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Switch it on to help Brain tell night from day.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed with this phone's key, through your call line.
  • Depends on: Brain, with mind.senses.phone.

app.senses.motion

Share whether you're still, walking or in a vehicle (worked out on the phone).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Switch it on so messages wait while you're on the move.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed with this phone's key; the accelerometer's readings never leave the phone.
  • Depends on: Brain, with mind.senses.phone; the physical activity permission.

app.senses.steps

Share your steps a day.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Switch it on if you'd like steps in Brain's picture of your day.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed with this phone's key, through your call line.
  • Depends on: Brain, with mind.senses.phone; the physical activity permission.

app.senses.light

Share the room's light: dark, dim or bright.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Switch it on to help Brain notice lights-out.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed with this phone's key, through your call line.
  • Depends on: Brain, with mind.senses.phone.

app.senses.places

Share arriving at and leaving your saved places (by name).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Switch it on so Brain knows when you're home or out.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Only the place's name, sealed; where it is never leaves the phone.
  • Depends on: Brain, with mind.senses.phone; places saved in the app.

app.senses.headphones

Share whether headphones are connected.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Switch it on to help timing (a call is fine with headphones in, say).
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed with this phone's key, through your call line.
  • Depends on: Brain, with mind.senses.phone.

app.senses.dnd

Share whether Do Not Disturb is on.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Switch it on so messages wait while your phone is on Do Not Disturb.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed with this phone's key, through your call line.
  • Depends on: Brain, with mind.senses.phone.

app.senses.alarm

Share when your next phone alarm is.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Switch it on if you set alarms on the phone itself rather than with Kurooto.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed with this phone's key, through your call line.
  • Depends on: Brain, with mind.senses.phone.

app.senses.wifi

Share which Wi-Fi you're on, as a code made on the phone, never its name.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Switch it on, then tell Kurooto once what a code is (/senses wifi label <code> home).
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Only the code, sealed; Android asks for location to read the Wi-Fi name, which stays on the phone.
  • Depends on: Brain, with mind.senses.phone; location permission.

app.senses.usage

Share your total screen time a day, never per app.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Switch it on if you'd like it in Brain's picture of your day.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Only the day's total, sealed, through your call line.
  • Depends on: Brain, with mind.senses.phone; Usage access in Android's settings.

Face presence

Kurooto learns your face, on this computer only, and greets you by voice when you sit down at your desk after a while. The camera looks at a few frames a second at most, only while you're not typing, and never keeps a picture: your face is a small encrypted template, a stranger's face is dropped with the frame, and nothing is sent anywhere. More in Face presence: greet you at your desk.

For every setting in this group, unless its entry says otherwise:

  • Costs and what leaves this computer: A little processor time while it watches (about 1-3% of one core), and the camera's light on while it looks.
  • Depends on: The Face presence feature, a camera, and Voice speaking for the greeting.

face.addons.known_guests

An add-on may say that everyone else in view is someone it knows: they get the short greeting instead of none.

A yes or no only: Face presence never learns who, and keeps nothing of it. With face.greet.with_others at skip, a remembered friend at the desk then gets "Welcome back." with no details and no conversation.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep skipping the greeting whenever anyone else is in view.
  • Depends on: An add-on that remembers people (with its own switch).

face.light_sense

Read the room's light from the camera's frames, as one smoothed number, for Brain and add-ons.

Only while the camera looks anyway: each frame's average brightness, smoothed, kept in memory as one number (dark to lit) and never a frame. A webcam adjusts its exposure, so it's a rough signal, not a light meter. Brain's sense of its body uses it when no other device reports its light; an add-on may use it to set a screen's brightness.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you have no phone or other device reporting the room's light.
  • Depends on: Face presence watching.

face.away.on

Notice when you leave the desk and come back.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you want calls and pop-ups held while you're away from the computer.

face.away.after_min

No sign of you (your face or the keyboard) this long means you've left, in minutes.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 5.
  • When to change it: Lengthen it if it decides you've left while you're reading.

face.away.use_places

Your phone leaving a place you saved marks you away from the desk at once.

Uses Brain's phone places sensor (the places you saved in the phone app). Leaving one makes Face presence decide you've left now instead of after a few minutes with no sign of you, so held calls and pop-ups start waiting sooner. Arriving somewhere doesn't mark you back: your face or your keyboard does. Only that the phone left and when are used, in memory; the place's name stays in the senses' store.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you walk away from the desk with your phone and want Kurooto to know straight away.
  • Depends on: Noticing when you leave (face.away.on), Brain on and its phone places sensor (mind.senses.phone.places).

face.away.places_close_camera

While your phone is out, keep the camera closed.

With use_places: from the phone leaving a saved place until it arrives at one, or someone types at the computer, Face presence pauses ("your phone is out"). Nothing watches an empty room then, so no arrival is seen by the camera and nobody else at the desk is noted.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you'd rather the camera rested while you're out.
  • Depends on: face.away.use_places.

face.away.report_others

While you're away, note the times someone else's face was in view at your desk, and tell you once when you're back.

Times and how many faces only, in memory, forgotten once told: never a picture, never who, never sent to your AI. Faces it isn't sure about (maybe you) don't count, and it only knows about the times the camera was looking.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you want to know whether someone used your computer while you were out.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: One message on your return when someone was seen.
  • Depends on: Noticing when you leave (face.away.on).

face.away.summary

Back at the desk: one "while you were away" pop-up, and a card on the dashboard's Waiting page with what came meanwhile.

The card lists the pop-ups held while you were away in full and the talk box's answers that came meanwhile, with Send now. The held pop-ups still show one by one, as before. In memory only.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on to see at a glance what you missed.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: One more pop-up when you come back to something waiting.
  • Depends on: Noticing when you leave (face.away.on).

face.away.summary_only

With the away summary: its one pop-up instead of every held pop-up; they wait on the card until Send now.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if the pop-ups on your return come all at once.
  • Depends on: face.away.summary.

face.away.hold_calls

While you're away, calls Kurooto would start by itself wait for you (up to the longest wait below).

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather a call rings the phone anyway.
  • Depends on: Noticing when you leave (face.away.on).

face.away.hold_desktop

Hold desktop pop-ups and spoken answers until you're back at the desk.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if others at the computer should still see Kurooto's pop-ups.

face.away.hold_max_min

The longest a call waits while you're away, in minutes.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 60.
  • When to change it: Shorten it for things that can't wait long.

face.camera.device

The camera to use: empty for the first ordinary one (infrared ones are skipped), or a number or part of its name.

  • Values: Text. Default: empty.
  • When to change it: Set it if you have several cameras and it picks the wrong one (kurooto face test-camera lists them).

face.camera.width

The picture width asked of the camera (it may give another).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 640.
  • When to change it: Lower it on a slow computer; recognition needs only a small picture.

face.camera.height

The picture height asked of the camera.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 480.
  • When to change it: Change it together with the width.

face.greet.on

Greet you by voice when you arrive at the desk.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you only want it to notice you, without speaking.
  • Depends on: Voice speaking.

face.greet.after_absent_min

Greet you only after you've been away at least this long, in minutes.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 20.
  • When to change it: Raise it if it greets you after short breaks.

face.greet.gap_min

The fewest minutes between two greetings.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 60.
  • When to change it: Raise it if the greetings feel too frequent.

face.greet.per_day

A daily cap on greetings.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 3.
  • When to change it: Lower it to one for just a morning greeting.

face.greet.say

What the greeting tells you: what's next today, what's waiting, deadlines in 24 h, what was held while you were away.

Parts: next, waiting, deadline, held, and (0.9.0) answers: how many answers weren't read aloud while you were away (a yes right after reads them). (0.9.1) missed_call, never on by default: a check-in call that went unanswered today; a yes takes it here, at the desk.

  • Values: A list, typed as JSON. Default: ["next", "waiting", "deadline", "answers"].
  • When to change it: Trim it to what you want to hear first when you sit down.

face.greet.tell_ai

When you answer the desk greeting, your AI is told what it had just said, so "yes" carries on from it.

Only with the first thing you say in the conversation the greeting opened, as a line of that turn's context. The greeting is rule-made: counts, and the title of your next class or a deadline (which your AI can read anyway); never anything the camera saw.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather the greeting's words never leave the computer; a bare "yes" may then need more words.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: A few dozen more words in that one request to the AI you picked.

face.greet.records

With Brain on, each greeting is one of its decision records, closed by whether you answered it.

It records which parts the greeting said (never its words or anything the camera saw) and, when the conversation opened, whether you said anything in it. The nightly look back reads how greetings went and may suggest a different gap between them (face.greet.gap_min); nothing changes without your tap.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't want greetings in Brain's records or the look back's suggestions.
  • Depends on: Brain, for the records; the nightly look back, for the suggestions.

face.greet.addons

An add-on may add one short line of its own to the desk greeting.

Said after everything else, only with nobody else in view, and at most 120 characters. At the greeting's length limit it's the first to go, so it never pushes out what's next or what waits. If the greeting didn't say it, the add-on says it its own way, as before.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on to hear an add-on's news (something it noticed while you were away) in the one welcome rather than separately.
  • Depends on: An add-on that uses it.

face.greet.talk

After the greeting, listen for a reply without its name being said.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather start every talk by saying its name.
  • Depends on: Voice listening.

face.greet.listen_s

How long that conversation stays open with nothing said, in seconds.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 12.
