  • When to change it: Shorten it if it keeps listening longer than you'd like.

face.greet.with_others

When someone else is in view too: skip the greeting, say just "Welcome back.", or the full greeting.

  • Values: skip, short, full. Default: skip.
  • When to change it: Leave it on skip so your day isn't read out in front of others.

face.tools

Your AI can ask kurooto_now: at the desk or away and since when, on a call, the camera's state, in words.

Also where Kurooto's messages reach you, Voice ID's last verdict as a word and Brain's plan line. Never a picture, who else is in view, a mood, Brain's read, tone or a sense reading.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if your AI shouldn't know whether you're at the desk.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Those words go to the AI you picked when it asks.
  • Depends on: Nothing; the desk and camera parts need Face presence.

face.track.speaker

While someone talks, the being's eye looks at them: at you when Voice ID says it's you, else the face moving most.

Only that someone is talking and Voice ID's word reach the dashboard, never the sound.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off for the eye that moves between faces by itself.
  • Depends on: face.track.on and Voice listening.

face.desk_time

Your time at the desk, from when Face presence saw you arrive and leave, in the evening check-in and the weekly summary.

"4 h 10 at the desk today, longest 2 h 40 unbroken": a total and the longest stretch, told once as your own numbers, never as a judgement or to check what you said you did.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you'd like to know how long you sit at the screen.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Those two numbers go where the check-in and the weekly summary go (your AI reads the call's script).
  • Depends on: Face presence.

face.desk_break

After a long stretch at the desk with no break, one message suggests a short one.

From Face presence's arrivals and departures (times only), once a stretch. It goes through Calm and Brain's door like anything Kurooto starts by itself, so it waits for a natural moment. Your own numbers, never a check on what you did.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you forget to get up.
  • Depends on: Face presence.

face.desk_break_min

Minutes at the desk in one stretch before the break suggestion (at least 15).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 90.
  • When to change it: Lower it for more frequent breaks.
  • Depends on: face.desk_break.

face.track.on

While the camera is open, follow every face in view (where it is and how much it moves) for the being's eye.

Each face gets a number that stays with it from frame to frame, its place, its size, whether it's you and how much it moves (small nods and turns). The being's eye looks at one person, then another every few seconds, sooner when someone new appears or moves more. Nobody else is identified, nothing is kept, and it never opens the camera by itself.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you'd rather the eye only finds you, or to save the processor time.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: With a dashboard in sight, roughly a tenth of one processor core while the camera is open.
  • Depends on: Face presence watching; a dashboard page in sight, or an add-on that follows faces.

face.track.fps

How many times a second it looks for faces while following them (1 to 15).

Only finding faces runs this often; recognising you stays at face.watch.fps. On a slow computer it lowers itself when a look takes too long.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 10.
  • When to change it: Lower it to save processor time; raise it for a livelier eye.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Each look a second costs about 1% of one processor core.
  • Depends on: face.track.on.

face.addons.watch

Let an add-on see who's in view: each face's place, size and numbers, in memory only, never a picture.

An add-on gets every face in each frame Face presence already looks at: where it is, how big, which way it's turned, whether it's you, someone else or unsure, and its numbers (kept by the add-on only if it says so). With no add-on asking, nothing is worked out for it.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to keep add-ons from seeing faces at all.
  • Depends on: An add-on that uses it; Face presence watching (every pause still applies).

face.addons.hold

Let an add-on keep the camera looking while you type (never past a pause, the night, a call or a switch).

Normally the camera closes while you use the keyboard and mouse. An add-on may ask it to keep looking, for at most 10 minutes at a time unless it asks again; the Face card and /face then say which add-on and why. Every pause reason still comes first. While it looks, other programs (a video call) can't use the camera on Linux.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if the camera's light on while you type bothers you, or you want the camera free for calls.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: The camera's light on, and a little processor time, while an add-on holds it.
  • Depends on: An add-on that uses it; Face presence watching.

face.learn.on

Slowly refine your face template from times it was very sure it was you (never unsure ones), with Undo.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if your look changes (glasses, a beard, the light at your desk) and you'd rather it keeps up.

face.learn.from_voice

A turn Voice ID is sure is your voice, with only your face in view, may teach the face model too.

Learning otherwise needs you to type within a minute of being recognised on arrival. With Voice ID on, a turn it is sure is your voice by the voice alone (not one your face helped decide), while exactly one face is in view, yours, and nobody else is about, is the same kind of confirmation: the clear, confident frame of you from the last 3 seconds may be learned from, with the same spacing, cap and Undo.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you mostly talk to Kurooto at the desk rather than type after sitting down.
  • Depends on: Learning (face.learn.on) and Voice ID.

face.learn.every_h

At most one new sample this often, in hours.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 6.
  • When to change it: Raise it to make the template change more slowly.

face.learn.margin

How far above your threshold a frame's score must be before it may refine your template.

  • Values: A number. Default: 0.08.
  • When to change it: Raise it to learn only from the surest frames.

face.learn.max

At most this many learned samples (the oldest goes first); the enrolled ones are never replaced.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 30.
  • When to change it: Lower it to keep the template closer to your enrolment.

face.looks.on

Add this look: you may add a short capture of how you look now (hair up, a new beard) to your face model.

When it says "unsure" because you look different today, the Face page's Add this look (or /face add-look) opens the camera for about 10 seconds and adds up to 10 samples of you as you are, as a look of their own, labelled and listed with Remove. Only you start it, alone in view, with enrolment's checks. A look too far from your face model to be sure it's you is refused with the reason; the threshold is never loosened for it. Off: no new looks, and the looks you added stop counting (they stay in your model until you remove them, and count again when it's back on).

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off to recognise you only by your enrolment (and anything learned).
  • Depends on: Face presence, enrolled.

face.recognise.frames

Recognised in this many of the last 4 frames means you're here.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 3.
  • When to change it: Raise it if it thinks you've arrived when you only passed by.

face.recognise.min_face_px

Faces smaller than this many pixels are ignored: too far away to judge.

Pixels of a 640x480 (or 480x640) picture; a sharper camera scales it (2.25 times at 1080x1440).

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 60.
  • When to change it: Lower it if you sit far from the camera.

face.recognise.strictness

How rarely a stranger may pass: loose (1 in 100 frames), normal (1 in 1,000) or strict (1 in 10,000).

  • Values: loose, normal, strict. Default: normal.
  • When to change it: Strict if others often sit at your desk; loose if it fails to recognise you in poor light.

face.recognise.threshold

0 uses the calibrated threshold; a score from 0 to 1 overrides it (never below 0.363).

  • Values: A number. Default: 0.0.
  • When to change it: Leave it at 0 unless the live test on the Face page shows it gets it wrong.

face.watch.fps

How many pictures a second it checks while waiting for you (0.2 to 2).

  • Values: A number. Default: 1.0.
  • When to change it: Lower it to save processor time; raise it if it's slow to notice you.

face.watch.fps_battery

Frames a second while the computer is on battery.

  • Values: A number. Default: 0.33.
  • When to change it: Lower it (or to 0) to save battery.

face.watch.look_fps

While Voice listens for a look (voice.listen.look_to_talk), the camera checks at least this many pictures a second.

A look opens the conversation after voice.listen.look_dwell_s of frames (by your eyes, at the first one: voice.listen.look_gaze_dwell_s), so a faster rate makes it react sooner. It applies only while Voice holds the camera to listen; otherwise face.watch.fps does. At most 5; a slow computer halves it as it does face.watch.fps.

  • Values: Frames a second, 0 to 5 (default 5; 0 keeps face.watch.fps). Default: 5.0.
  • When to change it: Lower it to save processor time; raise it if a look is slow to open it.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: A frame takes about 50 ms of one processor core at 1080x1440 (finding faces 14, recognising you 29, your eyes 7; a Ryzen 7 laptop), so 5 a second is about a quarter of one core while Voice listens.
  • Depends on: voice.listen.look_to_talk; Voice listening.

face.watch.idle_s

Seconds with no keyboard or mouse before the camera looks; while you type it doesn't need to.

  • Values: A whole number. Default: 60.
  • When to change it: Raise it to keep the camera off more of the time.

face.watch.night

Also look during the night hours (Availability's night); off pauses it at night.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you work at your desk at night and want the greeting then too.

face.watch.in_focus

Keep looking during focus and a study timer; off pauses the camera in focus, as before 0.9.0.

Look to talk, the desk keeping Kurooto awake, and an add-on's eye (the desk phone's face) go on while you study.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you want focus to mean no camera.

face.watch.when_locked

Also look while the screen is locked; off pauses it while locked.

  • Values: On or off. Default: off.
  • When to change it: Turn it on if you want it to notice you arriving at a locked computer.

face.phone_badge

The phone app's Today shows whether this computer's camera is on, and why.

Face presence's own line (looking for you, an add-on's hold by name, resting): words only, never a frame.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if you don't want that on your phone.
  • Costs and what leaves this computer: Sealed for each phone, with the rest of Today.
  • Depends on: Face presence and calls.today.

face.camera.yaw_offset_deg

How far beside the camera's lens the spot you look at sits, in degrees (+: to your right); look to talk centres there.

A camera whose lens isn't where you look (a drawn eye on a screen beside a phone's punch-hole camera, a webcam off to one side of a picture you look at) makes a look at that spot read as a turned head. This moves the centre of look to talk, by the head's turn and by the eyes alike, onto the spot. An add-on with such a camera may set it while its camera is in use and clear it after.

  • Values: -30 to 30 degrees; 0 (the default) is the lens itself. Default: 0.0.
  • When to change it: Set it if looking at the place you're meant to look rarely opens a conversation and looking at the lens does: about 7 degrees for 7 cm at 60 cm.

face.breaks

Leaving, looking up at the camera and standing up are natural breaks for Brain's door.

A message Brain holds until a natural break (mind.workspace.breaks) can go when Face presence sees you leave (with face.away.on), look at the camera for a second, or stand up (your face well under its usual desk size). Kinds and times only.

  • Values: On or off. Default: on.
  • When to change it: Turn it off if messages arrive at moments that don't feel like breaks.
  • Depends on: Face presence on and enrolled; Brain's mind.workspace.breaks.
